Let me compile the research and design document.\n\n---\n\n## Research Report: agtx TUI Architecture and Fleet Dashboard Design\n\n### Part 1: How agtx Renders the Kanban Board\n\n**Technology stack:**\n- `ratatui 0.30` for all terminal rendering (widget-based immediate-mode TUI)\n- `crossterm 0.29` for raw terminal input and alternate screen management\n- `tokio` async runtime for the event loop\n- `rusqlite` for per-project SQLite databases storing tasks\n\n**Rendering architecture** (from `/tmp/agtx-research/src/tui/app.rs`):\n\nThe TUI uses a standard ratatui immediate-mode pattern: a single `draw()` method called every iteration of the main loop, which calls `terminal.draw(|frame| ...)`. There is no retained-mode widget tree -- everything is redrawn from state every frame.\n\nThe main layout is a **three-layer vertical split**:\n\n```\n+-------------------------------------------+\n| Header (Length 3)                          |  <- Paragraph widget, project name\n+-------------------------------------------+\n| Board area (Min 0)                         |  <- 5 equal-width columns\n|  +-------+-------+-------+-------+-------+ |\n|  |Backlog|Plannng|Running|Review | Done  | |\n|  | card  | card  | card  | card  |       | |\n|  | card  |       | card  |       |       | |\n|  +-------+-------+-------+-------+-------+ |\n+-------------------------------------------+\n| Footer (Length 3)                          |  <- context-sensitive help text\n+-------------------------------------------+\n```\n\nWhen the sidebar is visible, a **horizontal split** wraps the entire layout:\n\n```\n+--------+-------------------------------------------+\n| Sidebar|  Header / Board / Footer                   |\n| (25ch) |  (Min 0)                                   |\n+--------+-------------------------------------------+\n```\n\n**Key widget choices:**\n\n1. **Columns**: 5 `Constraint::Percentage(20)` horizontal splits. Each column is a `Block` with `Borders::ALL`, title showing status name and count (e.g., `\" Running (3) \"`).\n\n2. **Task Cards**: Custom-rendered within each column. Each card is a fixed 10-row height `Block` containing:\n   - Line 1: title (truncated with `...`, bold, highlighted color when selected)\n   - Lines 2-8: description preview text (italic, dimmed)\n   - Border uses `BorderType::Thick` when selected, `BorderType::Plain` otherwise\n\n3. **Scrolling**: When tasks overflow a column, it computes `max_visible_cards = (column_height - 2) / card_height`, calculates a `scroll_offset`, and renders a manual scrollbar using `\"█\"` and `\"░\"` characters in a 1-character-wide strip on the right.\n\n4. **Popups**: Multiple popup layers rendered on top using `centered_rect()` helper + `Clear` widget to blank the area underneath. Popup types include:\n   - Shell popup (live tmux output)\n   - PR confirm popup (title + body editor)\n   - PR status popup (spinner/success/error)\n   - Diff popup (syntax-highlighted git diff)\n   - Task search popup (fuzzy search input + results list)\n   - Delete/Done/Review confirmation dialogs\n\n5. **Sidebar**: A `List` widget of `ListItem`s showing project names. Current project marked with `\" ●\"`. Selected item gets inverted colors (`bg = selected_color, fg = Black`).\n\n**Theming**: All colors come from a `ThemeConfig` struct with hex color strings parsed to `Color::Rgb(r,g,b)` at render time. Configurable via `~/.config/agtx/config.toml`.\n\n---\n\n### Part 2: How agtx Captures and Displays Live tmux Pane Output\n\nThis is the most architecturally interesting part (from `/tmp/agtx-research/src/tmux/mod.rs` and the shell popup rendering in `app.rs`).\n\n**tmux integration model:**\n\nagtx runs a **dedicated tmux server** named `\"agtx\"` (all tmux commands use `-L agtx`). This isolates agent sessions from the user's own tmux. The hierarchy is:\n- Server: `agtx`\n- Sessions: one per project (e.g., `\"my-project\"`)\n- Windows: one per task (e.g., `\"task-abc12345--my-project--fix-auth\"`)\n\n**Capturing output:**\n\nThe function `capture_tmux_pane_with_history()` at line 2736 calls:\n\n```\ntmux -L agtx capture-pane -t <window_name> -p -e -J -S -500\n```\n\nThe flags:\n- `-p`: print captured content to stdout (instead of a tmux buffer)\n- `-e`: include ANSI escape sequences (preserves colors)\n- `-J`: join wrapped lines (normalizes for varying pane widths)\n- `-S -500`: start capture 500 lines back into history\n\n**Before capturing**, the popup code resizes the tmux pane to match the popup's inner dimensions:\n\n```rust\nlet _ = std::process::Command::new(\"tmux\")\n    .args([\"-L\", tmux::AGENT_SERVER])\n    .args([\"resize-pane\", \"-t\", &popup.window_name])\n    .args([\"-x\", &pane_width.to_string()])\n    .args([\"-y\", &pane_height.to_string()])\n    .output();\n```\n\nThis ensures the captured content wraps correctly for the popup width.\n\n**ANSI parsing:**\n\nThe raw bytes from `capture-pane -e` are fed through a custom `parse_ansi_to_lines()` parser (line 2986) that:\n1. Walks each line character by character\n2. Detects `\\x1b[` escape sequence starts\n3. Parses SGR codes (Select Graphic Rendition) for colors, bold, italic, underline\n4. Supports 8-color, 256-color (`38;5;N`), and 24-bit RGB (`38;2;R;G;B`) modes\n5. Produces `Vec<Line<'static>>` with styled `Span`s that ratatui renders natively\n\n**Scroll model:**\n\nThe shell popup uses a `scroll_offset: i32` field. Negative values scroll up into history, zero means \"at the bottom.\" The code filters trailing empty lines, then slices the visible window from the parsed lines.\n\n**Refresh rate:**\n\nThe main event loop polls at `100ms` intervals (`event::poll(Duration::from_millis(100))`). Since tmux capture is called every frame during the `draw()` call when the shell popup is open, the live output effectively refreshes at ~10fps. This is a deliberate polling-based approach rather than push-based streaming.\n\n**Key forwarding:**\n\nWhen the shell popup is open, keyboard input that does not match a scroll or close binding is forwarded directly to the tmux window via `send_key_to_tmux()`. This lets the user interact with the running agent session from within the TUI overlay.\n\n---\n\n### Part 3: Keyboard Input Model\n\n**Input architecture** (from `input.rs` and `handle_key()` in `app.rs`):\n\nThe input system is a **layered modal dispatch**. The `handle_key()` method (line 1272) checks popups first, then dispatches based on mode:\n\n```\nPriority order (highest to lowest):\n1. PR status popup (blocks all input except Enter/Esc when done)\n2. Done confirmation popup\n3. Delete confirmation popup\n4. Review confirmation popup\n5. Diff popup (vim-like: j/k scroll, d/u page, g/G top/bottom, q close)\n6. PR confirm popup (Tab to switch fields, Ctrl+S to submit)\n7. Task search popup (typing filters, up/down selects, Enter jumps)\n8. Shell popup (Ctrl+q close, Ctrl+j/k scroll, all else forwarded to tmux)\n9. Dashboard mode (q quit, p projects, n new)\n10. Project mode with sidebar focused (h/j/k/l vim navigation)\n11. Project mode Normal (board navigation, task operations)\n12. Project mode InputTitle (text entry for task title)\n13. Project mode InputDescription (text entry with # file search)\n```\n\nThe input mode enum is simple: `Normal`, `InputTitle`, `InputDescription`. But the effective mode count is much higher because of the popup state machine.\n\n**Vim-like navigation** is the primary interaction paradigm:\n- `h/j/k/l` or arrow keys for movement\n- `o` to create (like vim's open)\n- `x` to delete\n- `m` to move forward\n- `/` for search\n- `Enter` to open/interact\n- `Esc` to go back\n- `q` to quit\n\n---\n\n### Part 4: Multi-Project Sidebar\n\n**Data source**: Projects are stored in a global SQLite database (`Database::open_global()`). Every time the user opens agtx in a git repository, that project is registered via `global_db.upsert_project()`.\n\n**Sidebar behavior**:\n- Toggle visibility with `e`\n- Fixed width: `Constraint::Length(25)` (25 characters)\n- Focus transfer: pressing `h`/Left from column 0 focuses the sidebar; pressing `l`/Right/Esc returns to the board\n- Navigation: `j`/`k` moves selection AND immediately switches projects (calls `switch_to_project_keep_sidebar()` which reopens the project database, reloads tasks, and ensures the tmux session exists)\n- Current project marked with `\" ●\"` suffix\n- Selected item renders with inverted colors (background = selected color, foreground = black)\n\nThe sidebar is architecturally simple -- it is just a `List` widget re-rendered every frame. The interesting part is that switching projects is instant because it swaps the SQLite database reference and refreshes the board state.\n\n---\n\n### Part 5: Fleet Dashboard TUI Design for 12 Proxmox Agents\n\nBased on the agtx patterns and your fleet-state.json structure, here is the architecture for a fleet-focused TUI dashboard.\n\n#### Data Sources\n\n| Source | Content | Refresh Method |\n|--------|---------|----------------|\n| `.fleet-state.json` | Agent IDs, VMs, IPs, tasks, branches, status | Read file every N seconds |\n| `gh pr list -R FinallyEve/pdn --json` | PR state, checks, review status per branch | Shell out to `gh`, poll every 30s |\n| SSH `tmux capture-pane` per agent | Last N lines of agent output | SSH + tmux capture, poll per-agent |\n| Prometheus `http://localhost:9090/api/v1/query` | CPU, memory per VM | HTTP query, poll every 10s |\n| Proxmox API via MCP | VM power state, uptime | API query, poll every 30s |\n\n#### Proposed Layout\n\n```\n+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n| FLEET DASHBOARD  Wave 6  |  12 agents  |  4 PRs open  |  14:35  |  <- Header bar (Length 3)\n+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n|          |                                                        |\n| AGENTS   |  KANBAN COLUMNS                                       |\n| (sidebar)|                                                        |\n| (20 ch)  |  +-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+     |\n|          |  |   IDLE    |  RUNNING  | PR SUBMIT |  MERGED   |     |\n| [01] Run |  |  (0)      |  (9)      |  (2)      |  (1)      |     |\n| [02] Run |  |           | +-------+ | +-------+ | +-------+ |     |\n| [03] PR  |  |           | |Agent02| | |Agent03| | |Agent09| |     |\n| [04] Run |  |           | |Smoke  | | |Termina| | |Regress| |     |\n| [05] Run |  |           | |Test   | | |l Recs | | |ion    | |     |\n| [06] Run |  |           | |CRIT   | | |  #47  | | |  #45  | |     |\n| [07] Run |  |           | +-------+ | +-------+ | +-------+ |     |\n| [08] Run |  |           | +-------+ |           |           |     |\n| [09] Mrgd|  |           | |Agent04| |           |           |     |\n| [10] Run |  |           | |Game   | |           |           |     |\n| [11] Run |  |           | |Stats  | |           |           |     |\n| [12] Run |  |           | |HIGH   | |           |           |     |\n|          |  |           | +-------+ |           |           |     |\n|          |  |           |   ...     |           |           |     |\n+----------+  +-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+     |\n|                                                                   |\n| DETAIL PANEL (Min 0, collapsible)                                 |\n| +---------------------------------------------------------------+ |\n| | Agent 04 - Game Statistics Dashboard                          | |\n| | VM 113 | 192.168.1.174 | 8 GB | CPU: 78% | MEM: 4.2/8 GB   | |\n| | Branch: vm-04/feature/game-stats-dashboard                    | |\n| | PR: none | Launched: 2026-02-14T20:32:30Z (18h ago)           | |\n| |                                                               | |\n| | --- Last 8 lines of SSH log ---                               | |\n| | > Analyzing src/game/stats.rs...                              | |\n| | > Creating dashboard component...                             | |\n| | > Writing tests for StatsCollector...                         | |\n| | > ✓ All 12 tests passing                                     | |\n| +---------------------------------------------------------------+ |\n+-------------------------------------------------------------------+\n| [j/k] select  [Enter] SSH  [l] log  [d] dispatch  [p] PR  [q] q |  <- Footer (Length 3)\n+-------------------------------------------------------------------+\n```\n\n#### Layout Structure (ratatui terms)\n\n```\nRoot: Vertical [\n    Header:  Length(3)       -- Paragraph\n    Middle:  Min(0)          -- Horizontal [\n        Sidebar: Length(20)     -- List widget (agent roster)\n        Content: Min(0)         -- Vertical [\n            Kanban: Percentage(55)  -- Horizontal [\n                Idle:     Percentage(25)  -- Block + List\n                Running:  Percentage(25)  -- Block + List of cards\n                PR Sub:   Percentage(25)  -- Block + List of cards\n                Merged:   Percentage(25)  -- Block + List of cards\n            ]\n            Detail: Percentage(45)  -- Block + Paragraph (selected agent detail)\n        ]\n    ]\n    Footer: Length(3)        -- Paragraph\n]\n```\n\n#### Widget Breakdown\n\n**1. Header Bar** -- Single `Paragraph` with inline spans:\n- Wave number (from fleet-state.json `.wave`)\n- Agent count summary (running/idle/pr/merged)\n- Open PR count (from gh query)\n- Current time (refreshed every frame)\n- Use `Style::bold()` for key numbers, dim for labels\n\n**2. Agent Sidebar** -- `List` widget, 20 characters wide:\n- One row per agent: `[NN] Status`\n- Color-coded by status:\n  - Idle: `Color::DarkGray`\n  - Running: `Color::Green`\n  - PR Submitted: `Color::Yellow`\n  - Merged: `Color::Cyan`\n  - Error/Unreachable: `Color::Red`\n- Selected agent highlighted with inverted colors\n- Priority badge: small colored marker for CRITICAL/HIGH\n\n**3. Kanban Columns** -- 4 columns (not 5; the fleet workflow is simpler than agtx's):\n- `Idle`: agents with no assigned task or completed task\n- `Running`: agents actively working (status \"running\" in fleet-state)\n- `PR Submitted`: agents whose branch has an open PR on GitHub\n- `Merged`: agents whose PR has been merged\n\nEach card (10 lines high like agtx) shows:\n- Agent ID + task name (bold)\n- Priority badge (CRITICAL in red, HIGH in yellow)\n- Branch name (truncated)\n- PR number and status if applicable (with checks status: green check, red X, yellow pending)\n- Last activity timestamp\n\n**4. Detail Panel** -- Large `Paragraph` block for the selected agent, containing:\n- Agent metadata (VM ID, IP, RAM, CPU/MEM from Prometheus)\n- Branch and PR info\n- **Live SSH log tail**: last 8 lines of output from the agent's Claude session\n- This is where the agtx `capture-pane` pattern adapts to SSH: `ssh -i key ubuntu@IP \"tmux capture-pane -p -S -8\"` (if the agent runs Claude inside tmux) or `ssh -i key ubuntu@IP \"tail -8 /tmp/claude-session.log\"` (if agents write logs)\n\n**5. Footer** -- Context-sensitive help bar, same pattern as agtx.\n\n#### Popup Overlays\n\nFollowing agtx's `centered_rect()` + `Clear` pattern:\n\n| Popup | Trigger | Content |\n|-------|---------|---------|\n| SSH Console | `Enter` on agent | Full tmux capture-pane with ANSI parsing, same as agtx shell popup. SSH tunnel to the agent VM, capture its tmux pane |\n| Log Viewer | `l` on agent | Scrollable log history with vim keybindings (j/k/d/u/g/G) |\n| Dispatch Work | `d` (global) | Task input form: select idle agent, enter task title/prompt, branch name auto-generated |\n| PR Detail | `p` on agent | Show PR diff, checks status, review comments (from `gh pr view`) |\n| Fleet Stats | `s` | Aggregate: total CPU, total RAM, tasks completed this session, PRs merged |\n\n#### Keyboard Input Model\n\nDirectly adapted from agtx's layered modal system:\n\n```\nLayer 1 (popups absorb all input):\n  SSH Console open     -> Ctrl+q close, Ctrl+j/k scroll, else forward to SSH\n  Log Viewer open      -> j/k scroll, d/u page, q close\n  Dispatch Form open   -> text input, Tab to switch fields, Ctrl+S to dispatch\n  PR Detail open       -> j/k scroll, q close\n\nLayer 2 (sidebar focused):\n  j/k                  -> move agent selection\n  Enter                -> SSH into selected agent\n  l                    -> open log viewer\n  h/Right              -> focus kanban board\n  e                    -> hide sidebar\n\nLayer 3 (kanban focused):\n  h/l                  -> switch columns\n  j/k                  -> select card within column\n  Enter                -> SSH into that agent\n  p                    -> view PR for selected agent\n  d                    -> open dispatch dialog\n  r                    -> refresh fleet state\n  /                    -> search agents by task name\n\nLayer 4 (global, always available):\n  q                    -> quit\n  1-9,0                -> quick-select agent by number\n  F5                   -> force refresh all data sources\n```\n\n#### Data Refresh Architecture\n\nUnlike agtx which captures tmux locally (instant), the fleet dashboard must handle SSH latency. Recommended approach:\n\n```\nMain event loop (100ms poll, same as agtx):\n  - Draw frame from cached state\n  - Check crossterm events\n  - Check async channels for completed data fetches\n\nBackground tasks (tokio::spawn):\n  - Fleet state reader:    read .fleet-state.json every 2s\n  - GitHub poller:         gh pr list every 30s\n  - Prometheus poller:     HTTP query every 10s\n  - SSH log tailer:        capture pane for SELECTED agent only, every 2s\n  - Proxmox status:        API query every 60s\n\nCommunication: mpsc channels from background tasks to main loop\n(same pattern agtx uses for pr_generation_rx and pr_creation_rx)\n```\n\nOnly tail the SSH log for the currently selected agent to avoid 12 simultaneous SSH connections. When the user switches agents, cancel the old tail and start a new one.\n\n#### Key Differences from agtx\n\n| Aspect | agtx | Fleet Dashboard |\n|--------|------|-----------------|\n| Scope | Single developer, multiple projects | Single project, multiple remote agents |\n| tmux access | Local tmux server | SSH to remote VMs, then tmux |\n| Data source | SQLite per project | fleet-state.json + GitHub API + Prometheus |\n| Card content | Task title + description | Agent ID + task + priority + PR status |\n| Columns | Backlog/Planning/Running/Review/Done | Idle/Running/PR Submitted/Merged |\n| Task creation | In-TUI with prompt editor | Dispatch dialog that SSHs and launches Claude |\n| Live output | Local tmux capture-pane | SSH + remote tmux capture-pane |\n| Sidebar | Project list | Agent roster (always 12 entries) |\n\n#### What Makes agtx's TUI Effective (and worth adopting)\n\n1. **Polling-based tmux capture is surprisingly good.** At 10fps, the live pane viewer feels responsive enough. The trick is resizing the remote pane to match the popup dimensions before capture. This same approach works over SSH with acceptable latency if you only tail one agent at a time.\n\n2. **ANSI parsing preserves color fidelity.** agtx's custom SGR parser (supporting 8/256/RGB color modes) means Claude Code's colored output renders correctly inside the ratatui overlay. This is critical for your fleet dashboard -- agent output includes syntax highlighting, progress bars, and status markers.\n\n3. **Layered popup state machine** avoids complex modal hierarchies. Each popup is just an `Option<PopupState>` field. The draw method renders whichever is `Some`, and the input handler checks popups top-down. This scales cleanly to the 6+ popup types your fleet dashboard needs.\n\n4. **Sidebar instant-switch** pattern is directly applicable. In agtx, moving the sidebar cursor immediately switches the project database and refreshes. For the fleet dashboard, moving the sidebar cursor should immediately update the detail panel and start tailing that agent's log.\n\n5. **The dedicated tmux server pattern** (`-L agtx`) is elegant for isolation. For the fleet dashboard, this maps to using a named SSH control socket per agent (e.g., `ssh -o ControlPath=/tmp/fleet-ssh-%r@%h`), keeping persistent connections that make repeated capture-pane calls fast.\n\n#### Recommended Technology Stack\n\nStay with the same stack agtx uses:\n- **Rust** with `ratatui 0.30` + `crossterm 0.29`\n- `tokio` for async SSH/HTTP polling\n- `serde_json` for fleet-state.json parsing\n- `reqwest` for Prometheus HTTP API\n- SSH via `std::process::Command` calling the `ssh` binary (same pattern as agtx's tmux calls -- shelling out is simpler and more reliable than an SSH library for this use case)\n- `gh` CLI for GitHub operations (same as agtx)"}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1566,"cache_read_input_tokens":70572,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1566},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"}},"requestId":"req_011CY9Tqy2wQy5zQwDdSeaqQ","type":"assistant","uuid":"7682deb8-d8a4-4acc-9dfc-7887c19096f7","timestamp":"2026-02-15T07:33:15.843Z"}
/Users/eviemini/.claude/projects/-Users-eviemini-Documents-code-server-manager/7688d2ab-634b-4274-a664-daabda403e5b/subagents/agent-a92c2a0ceb6001d8e.jsonl:{"parentUuid":"19fe0542-c0f9-422c-993e-2ce31d8d210b","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"7688d2ab-634b-4274-a664-daabda403e5b","version":"2.1.63","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a92c2a0ceb6001d8e","slug":"vectorized-exploring-steele","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LFh9ypokKzndg8qxbzjzoW","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Opening Sequence Proposals — Heroes of Mardov\n     2→\n     3→*Status: Three proposals for OQ-003 (\"What is the opening scene/chapter of the comic?\"). Evie decides.*\n     4→*Source: Full campaign read, style guide, VISION.md, DM notes.*\n     5→*Last updated: 2026-03-02, Director Agent*\n     6→\n     7→---\n     8→\n     9→## Context\n    10→\n    11→The opening sequence is the single most important creative decision in the series' launch. It determines:\n    12→\n    13→- **Who the audience meets first** — and therefore who they bond with\n    14→- **What tone is established** — gothic? adventurous? intimate? mythic?\n    15→- **How much worldbuilding comes before story** — the Mardov trap is over-explaining; the risk is under-explaining\n    16→- **Whether a newcomer stays or scrolls** — you have 30 seconds on TikTok, 2 minutes on YouTube\n    17→\n    18→The three proposals below offer different answers to the same question: *What makes someone who has never heard of Mardov want to know more?*\n    19→\n    20→Each proposal includes: the scene, why it works, what it establishes, how it hooks newcomers, and a panel-by-panel visual sketch.\n    21→\n    22→---\n    23→\n    24→## Proposal A: \"The Letter\"\n    25→\n    26→**Open on prophecy. Close on the heroes who must answer it.**\n    27→\n    28→### The Scene\n    29→\n    30→We don't start with the heroes. We start with Ruth — the Silver Chair of the Diviners Covenant — alone in her cottage, writing a letter by candlelight. Her voice narrates:\n    31→\n    32→> *\"The last war is coming and this world is not ready.\"*\n    33→\n    34→The camera pulls out. Through the window, we see a vast landscape — mountains, forests, frozen wastes, golden fields — all under a sky heavy with stars. Each star pulses, faintly, like a heartbeat.\n    35→\n    36→Cut to: the letter, sealed with wax, placed in the hands of a messenger. The messenger rides. We follow the letter across the world of Mardov in a sweeping travelogue — through the landscapes that will define each season. Each one is beautiful. Each one has something wrong. A shadow where there shouldn't be one. A fire that burns the wrong color. Frost in summer.\n    37→\n    38→The letter arrives. A hand opens it. We don't see the face — just the hands, the letter, and the words we've already heard.\n    39→\n    40→Cut to: a tavern. Strangers. The heroes, before they're heroes. They don't know each other yet. The letter has been read by someone who will bring them together. The music shifts from Ruth's solo cello to a full ensemble.\n    41→\n    42→Title card: **HEROES OF MARDOV**\n    43→\n    44→### Why It Works\n    45→\n    46→- Establishes the *cosmological* scope immediately — this isn't a dungeon crawl, it's an epic\n    47→- Ruth's letter is one of the most resonant source documents (RUTHS note.txt) — real DM writing, not adaptation\n    48→- The visual travelogue introduces the world without info-dumping\n    49→- Ends where the audience expects a story to start — strangers meeting — but with the weight of prophecy already in the air\n    50→- The letter becomes a recurring visual motif (letters appear throughout the campaign)\n    51→\n    52→### What It Establishes\n    53→\n    54→| Element | How |\n    55→|---------|-----|\n    56→| Tone | Gothic. Beautiful. Ominous. Warm candlelight against cosmic darkness. |\n    57→| World | Mardov as a living place — diverse landscapes, each distinct, each threatened. |\n    58→| Stakes | \"The last war is coming.\" From the first sentence, the audience knows something is at risk. |\n    59→| The Prophecy Structure | Ruth is a seer. The world has seers. Fate is known but not controllable. |\n    60→| The Heroes | Introduced last, as strangers — the audience meets them at the same time they meet each other. |\n    61→\n    62→### How It Hooks Newcomers\n    63→\n    64→A mysterious letter. A world that looks like a painting but feels like it's dying. Strangers who are about to become important. The audience wants to know: What is the last war? Why isn't the world ready? Who are these people?\n    65→\n    66→### Panel-by-Panel Visual Sketch (12 panels)\n    67→\n    68→| Panel | Visual | Audio/Text | Motion |\n    69→|-------|--------|------------|--------|\n    70→| 1 | Black. Then a single candle flame, center frame. | Silence. Then: the scratch of a quill. | Flame flickers (animated). |\n    71→| 2 | Pull out: Ruth's hands writing by candlelight. Old hands. Ink on paper. A cottage filled with dried herbs, star charts, crystals. | Ruth (V.O.): \"To whoever finds these words—\" | Candle flicker. Quill moving. |\n    72→| 3 | The letter, close-up. Ruth's handwriting fills the panel. Key phrase visible: \"The last war is coming.\" | Ruth (V.O.): \"The last war is coming and this world is not ready.\" | Ink drying on paper (subtle animation). |\n    73→| 4 | Through the cottage window: a landscape stretching to the horizon. Mountains in the distance. Deep indigo sky. Stars that pulse. | Ruth (V.O.): \"Four seals hold the darkness.\" | Stars pulsing (animated). Slow pan across landscape. |\n    74→| 5 | Ruth seals the letter with red wax. The wax imprint is a symbol — perhaps the Diviners Covenant mark. | The sound of hot wax dropping. A press. | Wax pooling and pressing (animated). |\n    75→| 6 | A messenger on horseback, letter in a satchel. They ride out of the cottage at dawn. | Music begins — a single cello. | Horse galloping, cloak billowing. |\n    76→| 7 | Wide shot: the rider crossing a golden field (Harvest landscape). In the distance, something shimmers — heat haze or magic. Behind: a shadow that doesn't belong to anything visible. | Ruth (V.O.): \"Four seals. Four hearts. Four anchors.\" | Grass blowing, shadow moving. |\n    77→| 8 | Wide shot: the rider passing a frozen lake (Winter landscape). The ice reflects a dragon — not present in the sky. | Ruth (V.O.): \"And the gods who hunger behind them.\" | Ice cracking subtly. Breath misting. |\n    78→| 9 | Wide shot: the rider through a dark forest (Forest landscape). The trees seem to lean in. Faces in the bark, half-formed. | Ruth (V.O.): \"I cannot stop what is coming.\" | Tree branches swaying. Shadow deepening. |\n    79→| 10 | Wide shot: the rider approaching a city built against a mountain (Mountain landscape). Forge-light from the mountain's base. | Ruth (V.O.): \"But I can warn those who might.\" | Forge-light flickering in the mountain. |\n    80→| 11 | Close-up: hands opening the letter. We see only hands — not the face. Firelight. A table. Other people nearby, out of focus. | Ruth (V.O.): \"Find them. Before the darkness does.\" | Letter unfolding. Firelight on paper. |\n    81→| 12 | Wide shot: a tavern interior. Multiple figures, faces shadowed. Each one distinct — a dwarf, a figure in robes, a cloaked silhouette, others. They don't know each other yet. Camera slowly zooms to center frame. | Music swells from cello to full ensemble. | Candle flames throughout the tavern. Ambient motion. |\n    82→| Title | **HEROES OF MARDOV** — text emerges from the center of the composition. | Full theme. | Title animation. |\n    83→\n    84→---\n    85→\n    86→## Proposal B: \"The Forge and the Axe\"\n    87→\n    88→**Open on a character. Close on the world that needs her.**\n    89→\n    90→### The Scene\n    91→\n    92→We start in darkness. The sound of a hammer on metal. Rhythmic. Ancient. The beat of a forge.\n    93→\n    94→Light blooms — orange, gold, white-hot. A dwarven forge. Sparks. Sweat. Hands that know iron. This is Thorkon's forge — Daina's father. Before the axe was the War Anchor. Before she knew.\n    95→\n    96→The camera follows the creation of an axe — not Daina's axe (or maybe it is — Evie's call). The forging process is shown with reverence: metal heated, hammered, folded, quenched. Each step is beautiful. The forge is not just a workshop; it's a temple.\n    97→\n    98→Daina is young, watching her father work. Focused. Reverent. This is her before the weight lands.\n    99→\n   100→The axe is finished. Thorkon holds it up — then hands it to Daina. It catches the light. For a single frame — one frame — the light on the blade shows something. A glyph. A pattern. The audience might not catch it the first time. It's the War Anchor sigil.\n   101→\n   102→Cut to: years later. Daina on the road, axe on her back, approaching a tavern where strangers wait. She's older. Different. The happiness from the forge is gone — or buried. But the axe is the same.\n   103→\n   104→She pushes through the door. Inside: the rest of the party. Strangers who will become family.\n   105→\n   106→Title card: **HEROES OF MARDOV**\n   107→\n   108→### Why It Works\n   109→\n   110→- Opens on a single character — the audience bonds with one person before meeting the world\n   111→- The forge sequence is visually stunning (fire, metal, Castlevania aesthetic, the style guide made manifest)\n   112→- Plants the Anchor mystery in the first 60 seconds, invisible to newcomers, devastating on rewatch\n   113→- The time-skip (young Daina → road Daina) immediately tells the audience: this person has been through things\n   114→- Daina is the War Anchor carrier — arguably the campaign's central character for the adaptation\n   115→\n   116→### What It Establishes\n   117→\n   118→| Element | How |\n   119→|---------|-----|\n   120→| Tone | Gothic. Warm but heavy. Beautiful craftsmanship with darkness beneath. |\n   121→| Character | Daina — a grounded, physical, emotionally complex hero. Introduced not as maker but as witness: her father Thorkon Fireforge forged the War Anchor axe. |\n   122→| The Axe | Central object of the series. Introduced as craft, revealed later as cosmological instrument. |\n   123→| Foreshadowing | The glyph flash. One frame. A promise to the audience that the show rewards attention. |\n   124→| The Heroes | Daina arriving at the tavern implies everyone else is already there. The audience enters through her eyes. |\n   125→\n   126→### How It Hooks Newcomers\n   127→\n   128→A master weaponsmith — Thorkon Fireforge — forging an axe, with his daughter Daina at his side. A time skip that says \"years passed and things changed.\" A mysterious flash that says \"there's more to this axe.\" A door opening onto strangers. The audience wants to know: What happened between then and now? What is that axe really? Who are these people?\n   129→\n   130→### Panel-by-Panel Visual Sketch (10 panels)\n   131→\n   132→| Panel | Visual | Audio/Text | Motion |\n   133→|-------|--------|------------|--------|\n   134→| 1 | Black. A rhythmic sound. Clang. Clang. Clang. | Only the hammer. No music. | None — still darkness. |\n   135→| 2 | A single point of orange light. Growing. A forge heating up. | The roar of bellows. | Embers rising (animated). |\n   136→| 3 | Wide shot: the forge. A dwarven workshop carved from stone. Tools on walls. An anvil center frame. A master weaponsmith — Thorkon Fireforge — working. Young Daina (16-18) assists nearby. | Hammer rhythm becomes musical. A slow, building percussion. | Sparks. Firelight. Sweat. |\n   137→| 4 | Close-up: metal on anvil. A blade taking shape. Sparks flying. | Hammer rhythm continues. | Sparks flying (animated). Vivid. |\n   138→| 5 | Close-up: Daina's face watching her father. Concentrated. Reverent. Lit from below by forge-light. | Percussion builds. A melody enters. | Firelight on skin. |\n   139→| 6 | The blade quenched in water. Steam. The metal singing. | A sustained note — the sound of the quench becomes music. | Steam rising (animated). |\n   140→| 7 | Thorkon presents the finished axe to Daina. She holds it up. It catches the light. Beautiful. Perfect. | Music peaks. A moment of silence. | Light traveling along the blade. |\n   141→| 8 | **The flash.** One panel — maybe even one second of animation — the light on the blade forms a pattern. A glyph. The War Anchor sigil. Gone before you can read it. | A single, dissonant note. Barely audible. | The glyph appears and vanishes. |\n   142→| 9 | Time skip. A road. Grey sky. A figure walking — Daina, years later. Axe on her back. Same axe. Different person. The happiness is gone. The determination remains. | Music is now a solitary violin. Weathered. Road-worn. | Cloak in wind. Boots on dirt. |\n   143→| 10 | A tavern door. Daina's hand pushes it open. Light and warmth spill out. Inside: silhouettes of the party. Faces not yet visible. | The violin opens into the full theme as the door opens. | Door opening, light pouring out. |\n   144→| Title | **HEROES OF MARDOV** | Full theme. | Title animation. |\n   145→\n   146→---\n   147→\n   148→## Proposal C: \"The Four Seals\"\n   149→\n   150→**Open on the myth. Close on the mortals it's about to consume.**\n   151→\n   152→### The Scene\n   153→\n   154→A creation myth, told visually.\n   155→\n   156→We see Mardov as it was — before the campaign, before the current age. The world in its making. Gods shaping continents. Light splitting into four colors. A voice narrates — not Ruth, not any character we'll meet. An older voice. Maybe the voice of the world itself.\n   157→\n   158→Four locations: A mountain forge. A primeval forest. A frozen throne. A harvest field in autumn. Four Zeniths.\n   159→\n   160→Something goes wrong. The gods war. Darkness rises. Guard'Daemon — the cyclic cataclysm — shown not as a monster but as an event. A wave. A silence. Civilizations falling in rapid succession, each one thinking they'd be the ones to survive.\n   161→\n   162→The four seals are placed. Four gods imprisoned. The world rebuilt on top of their prisons. A fragile peace built on four locks.\n   163→\n   164→Centuries pass in a montage. The seals hold. The world forgets why they were made. Cities grow. People live their lives. The danger fades into myth.\n   165→\n   166→Then: a crack. One of the seals. Almost imperceptible. A sound like ice breaking in a still room.\n   167→\n   168→Cut to: a tavern. Unaware strangers. The heroes, before they're heroes. Living their small lives in a world built on four prisons.\n   169→\n   170→Title card: **HEROES OF MARDOV**\n   171→\n   172→### Why It Works\n   173→\n   174→- Establishes the *entire mythology* in 90 seconds — the audience understands the world's foundation\n   175→- The visual creation myth is pure style-guide material (indigo, gold, crimson, grey stone)\n   176→- Guard'Daemon shown as a cycle, not a villain — immediately sets this apart from generic fantasy\n   177→- The \"crack\" at the end is the series' central dramatic question made into a single sound\n   178→- The transition from cosmic myth to small tavern creates the scale contrast that defines the series\n   179→\n   180→### What It Establishes\n   181→\n   182→| Element | How |\n   183→|---------|-----|\n   184→| Tone | Mythic. Ancient. The weight of deep time. Gothic at the grandest scale. |\n   185→| World | Mardov's entire cosmological structure — four Zeniths, four seals, Guard'Daemon. |\n   186→| Stakes | Civilizations have already died to this threat. The current world doesn't know it's next. |\n   187→| The Cycle | Guard'Daemon is not a person. It's a pattern. That's worse. |\n   188→| The Heroes | Introduced as tiny figures in a world that forgot its own danger. Maximum dramatic irony. |\n   189→\n   190→### How It Hooks Newcomers\n   191→\n   192→A creation myth that turns dark. Gods at war. A world rebuilt on four prisons. Centuries of forgetting. Then a crack. The audience wants to know: Which seal cracked? What happens when they break? And who are those people in the tavern who have no idea?\n   193→\n   194→### Panel-by-Panel Visual Sketch (14 panels)\n   195→\n   196→| Panel | Visual | Audio/Text | Motion |\n   197→|-------|--------|------------|--------|\n   198→| 1 | Black. Then: light. A single point of white light in void. | Narrator (V.O.): \"In the beginning, there was only the making.\" | Light expanding (animated). |\n   199→| 2 | The light splits into four colors: gold, green, ice-blue, amber. They spiral outward, forming a world. | Narrator: \"Four forces shaped this world. Four powers held it.\" | Colors spiraling, forming geography. |\n   200→| 3 | Wide shot: Mardov from above. Continents, oceans, mountains. Beautiful. Intact. The four colors visible as faint veins in the landscape. | Music: ethereal, choral, reverent. | Slow rotation. Clouds moving. |\n   201→| 4 | Four locations, rapid sequence: A mountain with fire in its belly. A forest so old the trees have faces. A frozen throne in an empty hall. A harvest field stretching to a golden horizon. | Narrator: \"Four Zeniths. Four hearts. Four seals.\" | Each location has signature atmospheric motion. |\n   202→| 5 | The sky darkens. The colors war — they collide, corrupt, consume. Gods as silhouettes, vast, fighting across the sky. | Narrator: \"And then the gods forgot why they were made.\" | Divine conflict — lightning, fire, shadow. |\n   203→| 6 | Guard'Daemon. Not a creature — a wave of nothing. An absence. Everything it touches ceases. Cities crumble. Light goes out. | Narrator: \"The silence came. As it always had. As it always would.\" | The wave of nothing advancing across the world. |\n   204→| 7 | Rapid montage: civilizations falling. One after another. Different eras, different peoples. All ending the same way. | Narrator: \"This was not the first time.\" | Quick cuts, each showing a different civilization's fall. |\n   205→| 8 | The four seals placed — four gods imprisoned. Each seal a different material: forge-iron, living wood, glacier-ice, autumn stone. | Narrator: \"They sealed the darkness. Not to destroy it — they could not. To delay it.\" | Each seal locking with finality. |\n   206→| 9 | Time-lapse: centuries passing. The seals buried under dirt, cities, forests, snow. The world growing on top of its own prisons. | Narrator: \"The world rebuilt. And forgot.\" | Seasons cycling. Cities growing. Rapid time-lapse. |\n   207→| 10 | A modern-day Mardov. Cities, trade routes, people living lives. No sign of the seals. No memory of the danger. | Music shifts: lighter. Normal. | Market sounds. Life. |\n   208→| 11 | Close-up: one of the seals, deep underground. Still. Silent. Holding. | Silence. | Nothing moves. |\n   209→| 12 | **The crack.** A hairline fracture across the seal's surface. The sound of ice breaking in a quiet room. | A single sharp sound — crack. Then silence. | The crack spreading (animated). |\n   210→| 13 | Pull out: above the seal, on the surface, life continues. No one heard it. No one knows. | A low, barely audible hum — the frequency of something waking up. | Normal surface activity. Oblivious. |\n   211→| 14 | A tavern. Warm light. Strangers at separate tables. They'll meet tonight, for reasons they don't yet understand. None of them heard the crack. | Music: the full theme, beginning quietly, building. | Candle flames. Conversation. Ambient tavern life. |\n   212→| Title | **HEROES OF MARDOV** | Full theme. | Title animation. |\n   213→\n   214→---\n   215→\n   216→## Comparison Matrix\n   217→\n   218→| Criterion | A: \"The Letter\" | B: \"The Forge and the Axe\" | C: \"The Four Seals\" |\n   219→|-----------|-----------------|---------------------------|---------------------|\n   220→| **Opens with** | Prophecy (Ruth) | Character (Daina) | Mythology (the world) |\n   221→| **Tone** | Intimate → epic | Personal → ominous | Mythic → intimate |\n   222→| **Worldbuilding** | Moderate (landscape travelogue) | Low (one forge, one road) | Maximum (full cosmology) |\n   223→| **Character introduction** | All at once (tavern) | One first, then others | All at once (tavern) |\n   224→| **Foreshadowing density** | High (letter content) | Extreme (Anchor glyph) | Moderate (the crack) |\n   225→| **Rewatch value** | Good (letter context deepens) | Excellent (glyph becomes devastating) | Good (knowing which seal cracked) |\n   226→| **TikTok clip potential** | Strong (Ruth's quote is shareable) | Strong (forge sequence is visual) | Moderate (needs context to land) |\n   227→| **Risk** | Could feel like a cold open that doesn't pay off quickly enough | Commits to Daina as \"the\" main character early | Could feel like an info-dump in disguise |\n   228→| **Style guide alignment** | High (candlelight, gothic, atmospheric) | Very high (forge-light, Castlevania, worn texture) | High (mythic scale, indigo/gold palette) |\n   229→| **Production complexity** | Medium (interiors, landscapes, characters) | Low-medium (forge interiors, one character) | High (cosmological visuals, time-lapse, divine conflict) |\n   230→\n   231→---\n   232→\n   233→## Director's Recommendation\n   234→\n   235→**Proposal B (\"The Forge and the Axe\")** is my recommendation for the following reasons:\n   236→\n   237→1. **Character-first storytelling.** VISION.md says \"the pacing and intimacy of a comic.\" Starting with a person making something with her hands is the most intimate possible opening.\n   238→2. **The Anchor glyph.** One frame of foreshadowing that pays off 20+ episodes later. This is the kind of detail that turns viewers into evangelists — \"go back and watch the opening again.\"\n   239→3. **Production feasibility.** A forge interior with one character is the simplest of the three to generate at the quality bar VISION.md demands. It lets us prove the style before attempting cosmic scale.\n   240→4. **Daina as entry point.** Her arc (mortal warrior → carries a god's prison → declares war on the gods) is the most complete character journey in the source material. She's the audience's way in.\n   241→5. **The time-skip.** Showing Daina young/happy and then older/weathered in two panels tells the audience everything about the series' emotional register without a word of exposition.\n   242→\n   243→However: **this is Evie's call (OQ-003).** If the campaign's emotional center is not Daina, or if Evie feels the mythology must come first, Proposal C gives the fullest world introduction. If the prophecy and Ruth's voice are the heart of the story, Proposal A gives the most atmospheric and narratively rich opening.\n   244→\n   245→All three end in the same place: strangers in a tavern, about to become heroes. The question is what the audience knows — and feels — before they get there.\n   246→\n   247→---\n   248→\n   249→## Open Questions for Evie (Related to Opening)\n   250→\n   251→1. Is Daina the right \"first face\" of the series? Or is another PC the emotional center?\n   252→2. Does Ruth appear in person, or only through her letter?\n   253→3. Should the opening sequence be the same every episode (like a TV intro), or unique to Episode 1?\n   254→4. Is there a moment from the actual first session (Chapter 0-1) that MUST be in the opening?\n   255→5."}]},"uuid":"a79a8975-0e11-422d-b854-513f75af4cfb","timestamp":"2026-03-04T22:45:12.374Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"19fe0542-c0f9-422c-993e-2ce31d8d210b"}
/Users/eviemini/.claude/projects/-Users-eviemini-Documents-code-server-manager/7688d2ab-634b-4274-a664-daabda403e5b/subagents/agent-a92c2a0ceb6001d8e.jsonl:{"parentUuid":"34fbc79c-f97f-45b7-930d-ba8780720cca","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"7688d2ab-634b-4274-a664-daabda403e5b","version":"2.1.63","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a92c2a0ceb6001d8e","slug":"vectorized-exploring-steele","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VVkxe4c8tJcCr5NWzJcTzc","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Mardov Visual Style Guide\n     2→\n     3→*Status: Working draft — art direction established, test shots pending.*\n     4→*Last updated: 2026-03-02, Session 01*\n     5→\n     6→---\n     7→\n     8→## The Tone\n     9→\n    10→**Gothic and dramatic. The Raven Queen's aesthetic.**\n    11→\n    12→Not grimdark for shock value. *Mythic* dark — the kind of darkness that has weight,\n    13→history, and beauty. Death is present but not ugly. Power is ornate. Suffering has\n    14→meaning. The world of Mardov has been through things. It shows.\n    15→\n    16→If the world had a color: deep indigo, aged gold, arterial red, and the grey of old stone.\n    17→If it had a texture: carved marble, worn leather, candlelight on water.\n    18→\n    19→---\n    20→\n    21→## Influences\n    22→\n    23→### Anime (Primary Visual Language)\n    24→\n    25→| Influence | What We Take From It |\n    26→|-----------|----------------------|\n    27→| **Studio Ghibli** | Environmental richness — backgrounds that breathe. Emotional expressiveness in character faces. The feeling that the world exists beyond the frame. |\n    28→| **Mushoku Tensei / Jobless Reincarnation** | Fine detail in clothing and world texture. The way light plays on different materials. Character weight and physicality. |\n    29→| **That Time I Got Reincarnated as a Slime** | Warmth within spectacle. Large-scale magic that feels joyful even when deadly. |\n    30→| **Sword Art Online** | Cinematic action staging. The way key moments are composed like film shots. Speed lines and impact that feel earned. |\n    31→| **The Rising of the Shield Hero** | Darker emotional register. Characters marked by what they've been through. |\n    32→| **Castlevania** | Gothic architecture and atmosphere. Dramatic high-contrast lighting. Fluid, violent action. Ornate, oppressive beauty. |\n    33→\n    34→### Western (Secondary / Structural)\n    35→\n    36→| Influence | What We Take From It |\n    37→|-----------|----------------------|\n    38→| **The Legend of Vox Machina** | Character-driven storytelling. Expressive, grounded character design. The feeling of real relationships under epic circumstances. |\n    39→| **The Mighty Nein (Critical Role)** | Moral complexity. The grey areas. Characters who are sometimes wrong and sometimes right. |\n    40→\n    41→---\n    42→\n    43→## The Synthesis\n    44→\n    45→We are not making a western comic with anime inspiration, or an anime with a western\n    46→production company's aesthetic. We're making something that grew out of a D&D table —\n    47→which means it lives in the space between those traditions.\n    48→\n    49→**The goal:** Anime's expressive range and detail density + western comic's dramatic\n    50→weight and compositional gravity + gothic atmosphere as the constant undercurrent.\n    51→\n    52→Every panel should feel like it could be a frame from a prestige anime series that\n    53→takes its world seriously. Every page should feel like it was designed to be *felt*,\n    54→not just read.\n    55→\n    56→---\n    57→\n    58→## Aesthetic Rules (Working — to be refined via test shots)\n    59→\n    60→### Color\n    61→- Deep, saturated palette — never washed out\n    62→- Key colors: indigo/violet (Mardov's sky and shadows), aged gold (magic, divine), arterial crimson (death, power), cold grey (stone, ruin, the undead)\n    63→- Warm candlelight against cold shadow is a recurring motif\n    64→- Magic should be visually distinct per school/source — establish and lock this early\n    65→\n    66→### Lighting\n    67→- High contrast — characters lit dramatically, not evenly\n    68→- Motivated light sources: torches, magic, moonlight, fire. Never \"ambient\"\n    69→- Rim lighting on characters in action scenes\n    70→- Ghibli-style soft diffusion in peaceful/natural moments\n    71→- Castlevania-style stark shadow in horror/dungeon moments\n    72→\n    73→### Line Weight\n    74→- Varied — thick outlines on characters, finer detail internally\n    75→- Environmental detail dense, character silhouettes clean and readable\n    76→- Inspired by the manga-influenced look of Mushoku Tensei and Castlevania\n    77→\n    78→### Character Design Principles\n    79→- Characters look like they've lived — wear, scars, expressions that carry history\n    80→- Clothing has weight and texture — armor feels heavy, robes flow\n    81→- Expressions are anime-expressive (wide range, readable) not western-flat\n    82→- Eyes especially — this is where emotion lives in anime, and we lean into it\n    83→\n    84→### Panel Composition\n    85→- Cinematic framing — treat every panel like a film shot (see `planning/pipeline.md`)\n    86→- Mix of wide establishing shots, medium character shots, extreme close-ups for emotion\n    87→- Negative space used deliberately — silence has visual weight\n    88→- Action panels: dynamic angles, motion blur, impact frames\n    89→\n    90→### Motion (Animated Panels)\n    91→- Subtle and purposeful — not everything moves\n    92→- Atmospheric priority: fire, smoke, fabric, water, wind in hair/cloaks\n    93→- Key emotional beats: a tear falling, a breath, eyes opening\n    94→- NOT full character animation — atmospheric motion that makes static art *breathe*\n    95→\n    96→---\n    97→\n    98→## What We Are Not\n    99→\n   100→- Not \"AI slop\" — every generation is directed, approved, iterated\n   101→- Not generic western fantasy — Mardov has a specific visual identity\n   102→- Not bright and cheerful — even in victories, there is weight\n   103→- Not uniform — different locations and moods should feel visually distinct\n   104→\n   105→---\n   106→\n   107→## Test Shot Priority\n   108→\n   109→Before any production panels, we generate test shots to lock:\n   110→\n   111→1. **A hero character portrait** — the closest character to Evie's main PC. Front, 3/4 view. This is the first real test of the style.\n   112→2. **An environment** — a key Mardov location. Establishes the world's visual vocabulary.\n   113→3. **An action moment** — one panel of combat or high drama. Tests staging and dynamism.\n   114→4. **A quiet moment** — two characters talking. Tests emotional expressiveness and lighting.\n   115→\n   116→All four must feel like they belong to the same world before we produce a single real panel.\n   117→\n   118→---\n   119→\n   120→## Open Style Questions (See OPEN_QUESTIONS.md)\n   121→\n   122→- Specific color hex values for the core palette (need test shots first)\n   123→- Magic visual system — how does each school of magic look?\n   124→- The Raven Queen's visual aesthetic — how do her servants/influence manifest visually?\n   125→- Day/night visual treatment — does the palette shift significantly?\n   126→"}]},"uuid":"6470e731-cb80-425b-8e82-59f076f5322a","timestamp":"2026-03-04T22:45:12.879Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"34fbc79c-f97f-45b7-930d-ba8780720cca"}
/Users/eviemini/.claude/projects/-Users-eviemini-Documents-code-server-manager/7688d2ab-634b-4274-a664-daabda403e5b/subagents/agent-a92c2a0ceb6001d8e.jsonl:{"parentUuid":"83d5fc75-0a33-4fc8-9130-82c24fcf96f7","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"7688d2ab-634b-4274-a664-daabda403e5b","version":"2.1.63","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a92c2a0ceb6001d8e","slug":"vectorized-exploring-steele","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014WrNessVomuaLEmFS3mrEv","type":"tool_result","content":"3:*The art direction rules for the entire series. Every panel, every frame, every generation prompt flows from this document.*\n18:| **Western Comics** | Dramatic weight, compositional gravity, bold panel layouts, strong silhouettes | Panel structure, character silhouettes, narrative pacing |\n28:- Every panel staged like a film frame from a prestige anime that takes its world seriously\n37:Every panel is designed. No throwaway frames. Negative space is intentional. Characters are placed within the frame with the deliberation of a film director. Reference: The Legend of Vox Machina's key frames.\n40:The world of Mardov has been through things. It shows. Light sources are always motivated (torch, moonlight, magic, fire — never ambient). Shadows are deep. Architecture is ornate and crumbling. Beauty and decay coexist in every frame. Reference: Castlevania Netflix.\n43:Backgrounds are not backdrops — they are characters. A room tells you who lives there. A forest tells you what happened here. The environment exists beyond the panel edges. Reference: Studio Ghibli's lived-in worlds.\n73:- Speed lines and impact frames use pure white against dark\n88:- Panel borders may break or dissolve\n117:Each major character has a signature color presence. When they enter a scene, their personal palette subtly influences the frame:\n175:Heroes of Mardov is read as a **vertical scroll** — panels stack vertically, and the reader scrolls down. This fundamentally changes composition from traditional manga/comics.\n178:- No double-page spreads — but we can use TALL panels that take the full scroll width\n187:| **Standard** | 1:1 to 3:2 | Dialogue, reaction shots, medium shots | 60% of panels |\n191:| **Vignette** | Circular, oval, or irregular border | Flashbacks, memories, visions, prophecies | 5% |\n192:| **Borderless** | Bleeds to edge, no panel border | Overwhelming moments — divine encounters, catastrophe | 5% |\n197:Every 3-5 panels should create a mini-narrative arc. The reader scrolls through setup → tension → payoff. Use the fold (what's just off-screen below) for reveals, punchlines, and dramatic entrances.\n203:The space between panels (gutter) controls pacing:\n210:The most common storytelling unit: three panels in sequence.\n211:1. **Setup panel** — establishes the situation (medium shot)\n212:2. **Focus panel** — the moment (close-up on face, weapon, object)\n213:3. **Impact panel** — the consequence (wide shot, reaction, aftermath)\n218:White (or black) space between panels, or large empty areas within panels, represent silence. Silence is a storytelling tool. A character standing alone in a vast dark space IS a panel. The absence of detail is a choice.\n242:- Standard: White with thin black border, slight round-rect shape\n243:- Whisper: Dashed border, smaller text\n244:- Shouting: Jagged border, bold text\n247:- Narration: Rectangular box, dark background with light text (positioned at panel top or bottom)\n251:Sound effects are DRAWN, not typed. They are part of the visual composition.\n253:**Style:** Bold, angular, integrated into the panel art — not floating on top of it. Inspired by manga SFX but adapted for the gothic aesthetic.\n256:- Impact sounds (CRASH, BOOM, CLANG) — large, jagged, fill panel space, warm/hot colors\n259:- Silence — the ABSENCE of SFX in a panel where you'd expect sound is a deliberate beat\n263:**Style:** Serif font in rectangular boxes. Positioned at the top or bottom of panels. Dark background (deep indigo or black) with cream/aged-gold text.\n273:Per DEC-007, panels are generated as static images first. Motion (via Kling) is added in post-production to select panels. Not every panel moves. Most don't. Motion is earned.\n288:- Standard dialogue panels\n292:- Anything where motion would distract from the composition\n296:Target: **8-15 motion panels per 5-minute episode**. This keeps motion feeling special while providing enough to distinguish the format from a static comic.\n301:- 1-2 action motion panels (the moments that make people gasp)\n303:- 1 signature moment (the panel that becomes the episode's defining image)\n326:Every generated panel is checked against:\n360:**Lighting Continuity:** If a scene is lit by torchlight, every panel in that scene uses the same light direction and color temperature. Characters don't spontaneously change which side is lit.\n362:**Background Continuity:** If a scene takes place in a specific room, the room's layout stays consistent panel to panel. Furniture doesn't move. Windows don't appear and disappear.\n372:The infinite vertical canvas means we control TIME through SPACE. More vertical distance between panels = more time passing in the reader's experience. This is the most powerful tool in the webtoon format.\n378:| **Combat** | Tight panels, narrow gutters, rapid succession. Occasional full-width impact panel that STOPS the scroll. |\n379:| **Dialogue** | Standard panels, standard gutters. Close-ups interspersed. Panel count matches conversation rhythm. |\n380:| **Emotional Climax** | Slow pacing. Wide gutters. Single-image panels taking full scroll width. Let the image breathe. |\n381:| **Montage/Travel** | Overlapping panels, fade transitions, compressed time. Many locations in few panels. |\n382:| **Horror/Tension** | Increasingly tall panels (the reader has to scroll further to see what's below). Dark gutters. Narrow panels that feel claustrophobic. |\n383:| **Comedy** | Quick cuts, small reaction panels, exaggerated expressions. Tight timing. The punchline lands on the scroll reveal. |\n384:| **Revelation** | Maximum use of the scroll reveal. Long setup. Then the payoff panel appears as they scroll. Full-width, high detail, maximum impact. |\n388:Each episode (5-15 minutes as video, ~60-120 panels as comic) follows a pacing arc:\n391:Opening (5-10 panels)       — Establish mood, location, state of play\n394:Rising Action (15-30 panels) — Conflict introduced, tension builds\n397:Climax (5-15 panels)        — The peak moment\n398:                               Most motion panels concentrate here\n399:                               Full-width panels, dramatic composition\n401:Falling Action (10-20 panels)— Aftermath, emotional processing\n405:Close (3-5 panels)          — Final image, cliffhanger, or quiet beat\n406:                               Often a single powerful panel\n407:                               Sometimes a motion panel (atmospheric loop)\n447:Every major location in Mardov has a distinct visual identity that a reader could recognize from a single panel — even without characters or text.\n470:The panel border itself carries meaning:\n477:| **None (Borderless)** | Image bleeds to edges | Overwhelming moment — the panel can't contain it |\n\n[Showing results with pagination = limit: 60, offset: 0]"}]},"uuid":"23e94261-e08f-421c-a223-5dd713be5609","timestamp":"2026-03-04T22:45:27.325Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"83d5fc75-0a33-4fc8-9130-82c24fcf96f7"}

Each seal is a different material matching its Zenith. **Forge-iron** (Mountain's Seal) -- rough, dark metal still warm from the forge, glowing at its runic edges. **Living wood** (Forest's Seal) -- bark and root woven into a circular seal, green light pulsing faintly at its heartwood center. **Glacier-ice** (Winter's Seal) -- crystalline, refracting blue-white light, cold vapor rising from its surface. **Autumn stone** (Harvest's Seal) -- warm amber-brown stone veined with gold, grain patterns carved into its face. The four seals are arranged in a diamond pattern, being set into the raw earth. The ground around them is scorched and dead -- the aftermath of Guard'Daemon. This is an act of last resort, not triumph. The gods are sealing the darkness because they cannot destroy it. The mood is bittersweet: this is hope, but it is also an admission of failure.\n\n**Narration**: \"They sealed the darkness. Not to destroy it -- they could not. To delay it.\"\n*Font: Gothic serif | Position: Bottom of panel | Style: Muted gold (#b8860b) on dark earth brown -- the gold is not bright, not triumphant, but worn*\n\n**Color Palette**:\n- `#8b4513` Scorched Earth -- the dead ground receiving the seals\n- `#b8860b` Worn Gold -- the divine residue, tired, spent\n- `#708090` Forge Iron -- the Mountain's seal\n- `#2d4a22` Living Green -- the Forest's seal, faint heartbeat\n- `#c8dbe6` Glacier Blue -- the Winter's seal\n- `#DAA520` Autumn Amber -- the Harvest's seal\n\n**Lighting**: Each seal emits its own faint glow -- the last divine energy being poured into containment. The surrounding earth is dark. Overhead, the sky is the grey of aftermath -- no sun, no stars, just ash-filtered nothing. The only light in the world is coming from the seals themselves.\n\n**Motion Notes**: The seals descend slowly into the earth. The ground closes over them. Light dims as they sink below the surface. Dust settles. This is finality.\n\n**MJ Prompt**: `Four massive ornate seals being pressed into scorched earth by unseen divine hands, each seal a different material, forge-iron with glowing runic edges, living bark and root with green heartwood pulse, crystalline glacier-ice refracting blue-white light with cold vapor, amber-brown autumn stone veined with gold and grain patterns, arranged in a diamond pattern, dead scorched ground around them, ash-grey sky above, gothic anime synthesis, the seals glow faintly as the last divine energy is spent, bittersweet sacrifice not triumph, the gods sealing what they cannot destroy, muted earth tones --ar 9:16 --niji 7 --style raw --sref TBD`\n\n**Art Status**: EXISTING -- frame 04 U1 (four-seals-04-seals-placed, hash `5b60de9a`)\n\n---\n\n## Panel 9: Centuries Passing\n\n**Visual**: A single tall panel showing the passage of deep time through vertical layers. At the top, the bare scorched earth with the faint glow of a seal just barely visible. Moving downward through the panel (downward = forward in time): dirt accumulates. Grass grows. Roots thread through soil. A sapling becomes a tree in successive vertical stages. Stone foundations are laid. Walls rise. A village becomes a town. A town becomes a city. At the very bottom of the panel, the city is thriving -- market stalls, bridges, chimneys smoking, life everywhere. And deep, deep below the city (shown as a cross-section of earth at the bottom edge), the faint outline of a seal is barely visible, buried and forgotten. The world has literally grown on top of its own prison.\n\n**Narration**: \"The world rebuilt. And forgot.\"\n*Font: Gothic serif | Position: Bottom, just above the buried seal cross-section | Style: Desaturated cream on dark earth -- almost invisible, like a memory fading*\n\n**Color Palette**:\n- `#8b4513` Raw Earth -- the top layers, scorched, barren\n- `#6b8e23` Growing Green -- grass, saplings, life returning\n- `#a0522d` Warm Brown -- foundations, wood, construction\n- `#DAA520` Market Amber -- the thriving city at the bottom\n- `#4a6741` Deep Soil Green -- the deep earth layers hiding the seal\n\n**Lighting**: The panel transitions from the ash-grey overcast of aftermath (top) to warm golden sunlight (bottom). The light warms as you scroll down through time. By the bottom, the city is bathed in the kind of warm amber light that says \"this is a good place to live.\" Below the surface, the seal's glow is cold, forgotten, barely there.\n\n**Motion Notes**: Time-lapse compressed into a still image. If animated: seasons cycling rapidly through the layers -- snow, bloom, summer, autumn, repeating. Growth accelerating. The city assembling itself. But the seal below stays perfectly, ominously still.\n\n**MJ Prompt**: `Vertical cross-section showing the passage of centuries in a single tall panel, top layer is scorched barren earth with a faint seal glow, moving down through layers dirt accumulates and grass grows and roots spread and saplings become trees, stone foundations are laid and walls rise, a village becomes a town becomes a thriving medieval fantasy city with market stalls and bridges at the bottom, deep below the city in a cross-section of earth the faint forgotten outline of an ancient seal barely visible, gothic anime synthesis, deep time geological layering, warm light increasing from ashen top to golden-amber bottom, life growing on top of its own prison --ar 9:16 --niji 7 --style raw --sref TBD`\n\n**Art Status**: NEEDS GENERATION\n\n---\n\n## Panel 10: Modern Mardov\n\n**Visual**: Eye-level view of a living, breathing fantasy city -- what Mardov looks like now, in the age before the campaign begins. A market square: vendors hawking wares from wooden stalls. A blacksmith's hammer rings. Children chase each other between the legs of adults. A dwarf inspects a blade. A hooded figure passes through the crowd without being noticed. Banners hang from stone walls. The architecture is varied -- human timber-and-stone, dwarven carved arches, hints of elven filigree on older buildings. Smoke from chimneys. Laundry drying on lines. The light is warm, the colors are alive, and NO ONE is thinking about what is buried below their feet. This is normalcy. This is the world the heroes will be asked to save.\n\n**Narration**: *No narration. Let the warmth and normalcy breathe. The contrast with what came before -- and what is about to come -- does the work.*\n\n**Color Palette**:\n- `#DAA520` Market Gold -- warm amber sunlight, stall fabrics, grain\n- `#a0522d` Timber Brown -- buildings, carts, barrels\n- `#708090` Stone Grey -- the older architecture, walls, cobblestones\n- `#c41e3a` Banner Crimson -- splashes of color in flags and fabrics\n- `#4a7c3f` Living Green -- vines on walls, potted herbs, life\n\n**Lighting**: Warm afternoon sunlight filtering through streets. Natural, generous, golden-hour quality. Soft shadows. For the first time in the sequence, the lighting is simply... pleasant. Motivated by the sun. This is how the world looks when it has forgotten its own danger.\n\n**Motion Notes**: Market bustle. Smoke rising. Children running. A dog following its owner. Banners fluttering in a gentle breeze. Everything is alive, warm, ordinary. The motion is casual, unhurried, unafraid.\n\n**MJ Prompt**: `Eye-level view of a bustling medieval fantasy market square in warm afternoon sunlight, vendors at wooden stalls selling wares, a blacksmith hammering, children running between adults, a dwarf inspecting a blade, banners hanging from stone walls, mixed architecture of human timber-and-stone and dwarven carved arches and hints of elven filigree, smoke from chimneys, laundry on lines, warm golden-amber light, gothic anime synthesis, the city is alive and oblivious and beautiful, no danger visible, normalcy and warmth, detailed atmospheric scene with many small figures living ordinary lives --ar 9:16 --niji 7 --style raw --sref TBD`\n\n**Art Status**: NEEDS GENERATION\n\n---\n\n## Panel 11: Seal Underground\n\n**Visual**: Total tonal shift. We plunge below the surface. An extreme close-up of one of the four seals, deep underground, entombed in rock and earth and the roots of a thousand years of growth. The seal is massive -- carved with runes that no living person can read. It is made of forge-iron (the Mountain's Seal / Anchor of War). Its surface is dark, rough, ancient. It has not moved in centuries. It has not needed to. Around it, the underground is silent. No creatures burrow here. No roots reach this deep. The earth itself avoids the seal, leaving a thin gap of dead air around its edges. The only light is the seal's own faint residual glow -- cold, blue-green, barely there. Like a heartbeat monitor for a patient who might already be dead.\n\n**Narration**: *Silence. No narration, no sound. The absence of sound IS the statement. After the warmth and noise of Panel 10, this silence is deafening.*\n\n**Color Palette**:\n- `#1a3a2e` Deep Underground Teal -- the dominant color, cold, buried\n- `#2d4a3a` Dark Earth Green -- the soil and rock around the seal\n- `#708090` Ancient Iron -- the seal itself, tarnished, dark\n- `#4a7c6f` Cold Residual Glow -- the seal's faint luminescence\n- `#000000` Dead Air Black -- the gap between seal and earth\n\n**Lighting**: Almost none. Through the crack, a different light bleeds: not the seal's cold blue-green, but a deep, angry red-orange. The light of what is sealed inside. The red light seeps through the crack like blood through a wound, staining the surrounding rock, casting impossible shadows in the underground chamber. Small fragments of rune-carved iron have flaked away from the crack's edges. The dead air gap around the seal has widened -- the earth itself is pulling away. Something is wrong. Something has shifted. The seal held for a thousand years. It is not holding now.\n\n**Narration**: *A single sound effect, not narration: CRACK. Drawn into the panel in angular, shattering letterforms. Then silence.*\n*If text narration is needed: \"...\" -- three dots, positioned below the crack, in red (#c41e3a) on black*\n\n**Color Palette**:\n- `#1a3a2e` Deep Underground Teal -- the familiar chamber\n- `#c41e3a` Crack Red -- the angry light bleeding through\n- `#8b0000` Deep Crimson -- the deeper glow within\n- `#708090` Ancient Iron -- the seal's surface, now fractured\n- `#000000` Widening Void -- the gap between seal and earth growing\n\n**Lighting**: Two light sources now. The same city from Panel 10 -- the same market square, the same warmth, the same people living their lives. But something is different, and the audience should feel it before they can name it. A shadow falls where there should not be one -- not cast by any building or cloud. A child's laughter trails off mid-sound (implied by the child's expression shifting). A dog that was running in Panel 10 now stands rigid, hackles raised, staring at the ground. A vendor's candle flame leans sideways despite no wind. The colors are very slightly desaturated compared to Panel 10 -- 5-10% shifted toward grey, barely noticeable. The warm amber light has a faint cool undertone. No one has noticed yet. No one will notice until it is too late. The dread is below the threshold of consciousness.\n\n**Narration**: \"No one heard it. No one knew.\"\n*Font: Gothic serif | Position: Bottom of panel | Style: Muted grey (#a9a9a9) on dark overlay -- the text itself is quiet, almost apologetic*\n\n**Color Palette**:\n- `#C8A830` Slightly Dulled Gold -- the same market amber, but 10% desaturated\n- `#9a8a7a` Warm Grey -- the stone walls losing their warmth\n- `#708090` Creeping Grey -- the shadow that should not be there\n- `#a0522d` Timber Brown -- still warm, but cooling\n- `#3a3a3a` Wrong Shadow -- the shadow cast by nothing\n\n**Lighting**: Almost identical to Panel 10, but with a subtle wrongness. Warm candlelight on worn wood. The scene is intimate after the cosmic scale of what came before -- the camera has come all the way down from gods and seals and civilizations to a single room. Five to seven figures sit at separate tables. They do not know each other. A dwarf with red hair and an axe on her back (Daina) sits alone, staring into an ale. A cloaked figure in dark robes (Nocturnus) watches the room from a corner, hood up. A small gnome (Sprocket) tinkers with something mechanical at the bar. A tiefling with blue-pink coloring (Nyx) laughs with a stranger, performing, hiding. A broad figure with ember-orange undertones (Bergs) sits with his back to the wall, arms crossed. Others are present but less defined -- shadows and silhouettes. The fire in the hearth burns warm. The mugs are full. Outside the window, the sky is ordinary. None of these people heard the crack. None of them know that the world they are sitting in is built on four prisons that are starting to fail. They are about to become heroes. [Genesis]\nPanel 2:  Gold, green, ice-blue, amber     [Four forces distinct]\nPanel 3:  Full warm spectrum, pristine      [World at its most beautiful]\nPanel 4:  Each Zenith's signature color unknowing]\n```\n\n---\n\n## Panel 1: The Making\n\n**Visual**: Total darkness. Then a single point of white light blooms at center frame, impossibly bright, expanding outward into warm gold. The light is not a star -- it is the act of creation itself, raw and radiant. Faint geometric patterns (the Rose of Creation) shimmer at the edges of the expanding glow, hinting at petals, at structure, at intention. The void around it is not empty -- it is waiting.\n\n**Narration**: \"In the beginning, there was only the making.\"\n*Font: Gothic serif, small caps | Position: Bottom center | Style: Cream (#F5F0E1) on void black, faint gold (#D4A017) drop shadow*\n\n**Color Palette**:\n- #000000 Void Black -- the nothing before creation\n- #FFFFFF Genesis White -- the first light\n- #D4A017 Aged Gold -- divine warmth bleeding in\n- #1a1a3e Deep Indigo -- the edges of void just touched by light\n- #F5F0E1 Parchment Cream -- the narration color\n\n**Lighting**: Single radial source, center frame. Light IS the subject. No shadows -- shadow has not been invented yet. The gold warmth increases as the light expands outward.\n\n**Motion Notes**: Light expanding outward in a slow bloom. Geometric Rose patterns pulsing faintly at the edges. The void is still. The light is alive.\n\n**MJ Prompt**: `A single point of white light blooming in cosmic void, expanding outward into warm gold radiance, faint geometric petal patterns shimmering at the edges of the glow, the act of creation itself, gothic anime synthesis, deep indigo void giving way to blinding divine gold, no stars only pure genesis light, vertical composition, ethereal and mythic, high detail on light refraction and geometric patterns --ar 9:16 --niji 7 --style raw --sref TBD`\n\n**Art Status**: EXISTING -- frame 01 U2 (four-seals-01-the-making, hash 67a15e7c)\n\n---\n\n## Panel 2: Four Forces\n\n**Visual**: The genesis light has split into four distinct streams of color spiraling outward like ribbons of power. Gold (forge-fire, the Mountain's force), deep emerald green (living growth, the Forest's force), ice-blue crystalline light (the frozen clarity of Winter), and warm harvest amber (the fertile abundance of the fields). The four colors braid and twist around each other but do not merge -- each is sovereign, each is necessary. Where they pass, matter forms: rock, water, root, grain. The suggestion of a world taking shape from raw energy.\n\n**Narration**: \"Four forces shaped this world. Four powers held it.\"\n*Font: Gothic serif, small caps | Position: Bottom center | Style: Cream (#F5F0E1) on translucent dark overlay*\n\n**Color Palette**:\n- #D4A017 Forge Gold -- Mountain's Zenith, power of war and making\n- #2d4a22 Forest Moss -- Forest's Zenith, power of life and death\n- #c8dbe6 Frost White-Blue -- Winter's Zenith, power of faith and endurance\n- #DAA520 Harvest Amber -- Harvest's Zenith, power of abundance and covenant\n- #1a1a3e Deep Indigo -- the void being displaced\n\n**Lighting**: Four light sources spiraling outward from a shared origin point. Each color casts its own glow. The indigo void retreats before them. No single dominant source -- the four share dominion.\n\n**Motion Notes**: The four ribbons spiral slowly, braiding around each other. Matter crystallizes where they pass -- sparks of creation.
