That is what makes it terrifying.\n\n**MJ Prompt**: `A medieval fantasy market square in warm afternoon light nearly identical to a previous lively scene but with subtle wrongness, a shadow falling where no building casts it, a dog standing rigid with hackles raised staring at the ground, a candle flame leaning sideways despite no wind, colors slightly desaturated 10 percent toward grey compared to normal, the warm golden light has a faint cool undertone, vendors still selling and people still walking but three points of frozen unease in the crowd, gothic anime synthesis, surface normalcy hiding subconscious dread, the moment just after something terrible happened below and no one knows yet, barely perceptible horror --ar 9:16 --niji 7 --style raw --sref TBD`\n\n**Art Status**: NEEDS GENERATION\n\n---\n\n## Panel 14: The Tavern\n\n**Visual**: Interior of a tavern. Warm candlelight on worn wood. The scene is intimate after the cosmic scale of what came before -- the camera has come all the way down from gods and seals and civilizations to a single room. Five to seven figures sit at separate tables. They do not know each other. A dwarf with red hair and an axe on her back (Daina) sits alone, staring into an ale. A cloaked figure in dark robes (Nocturnus) watches the room from a corner, hood up. A small gnome (Sprocket) tinkers with something mechanical at the bar. A tiefling with blue-pink coloring (Nyx) laughs with a stranger, performing, hiding. A broad figure with ember-orange undertones (Bergs) sits with his back to the wall, arms crossed. Others are present but less defined -- shadows and silhouettes. The fire in the hearth burns warm. The mugs are full. Outside the window, the sky is ordinary. None of these people heard the crack. None of them know that the world they are sitting in is built on four prisons that are starting to fail. They are about to become heroes. They do not know that either.\n\n**Narration**: \"And below them, the world held its breath.\"\n*Font: Gothic serif | Position: Very bottom of the panel, below the tavern floor line | Style: Faint red (#8b0000) on black -- the color of the crack from Panel 12, bleeding upward into the warmth*\n\n**Color Palette**:\n- #DAA520 Candlelight Amber -- the dominant light, warm, intimate\n- #a0522d Tavern Wood -- tables, beams, bar\n- #8b4513 Dark Wood -- the shadows of the tavern's corners\n- #D4A017 Firelight Gold -- the hearth, the warm center\n- #8b0000 Hidden Crimson -- a faint red undertone at the very bottom edge, barely visible\n\n**Lighting**: Candlelight and hearth-fire. Multiple warm light sources filling the room with the kind of amber glow that makes strangers feel safe. This is the candlelight-vs-shadow motif from the style guide -- the small warm thing that persists against the dark. Rim lighting on the figures from the hearth, casting them in the warm-but-shadowed register that defines the series' look.\n\n**Motion Notes**: Candle flames flicker. Hearth fire crackles. Steam rises from mugs. Sprocket's hands move over their project. Nyx gestures while talking. Everyone else is still -- waiting, without knowing they are waiting. The ambient motion is tavern-life: warm, human, alive.\n\n**MJ Prompt**: `Interior of a medieval fantasy tavern in warm candlelight and hearth-fire glow, five to seven distinct figures sitting at separate tables who do not know each other, a red-haired dwarf woman with an axe on her back staring into ale, a cloaked dark figure watching from a shadowed corner, a small gnome tinkering with mechanical parts at the bar, a blue-pink tiefling laughing performatively, a broad iron-and-ember figure sitting with arms crossed back to wall, warm amber light on worn wood tables and beams, the hearth burning gold at center, gothic anime synthesis, intimate scale after cosmic myth, strangers who are about to become heroes, candlelight against shadow, dramatic rim lighting on each figure, a faint barely-visible red undertone at the very bottom edge of the panel --ar 9:16 --niji 7 --style raw --sref TBD`\n\n**Art Status**: NEEDS GENERATION\n\n---\n\n## Post-Panel 14: Title Card\n\n**HEROES OF MARDOV**\n\n*Font: Custom gothic serif, ornate | Style: Gold (#D4A017) on deep indigo (#1a1a3e), faint Rose of Creation pattern in the background*\n\n*Music: Full theme begins, building from the quiet tavern ambience.*\n\n---\n\n## Production Notes\n\n### Panel Prioritization\n1. **Panel 5 (Divine Conflict)** -- Most complex, most important, will require multiple iterations. Generate first and iterate.\n2. **Panel 14 (The Tavern)** -- Introduces the heroes. Must match their character designs (pending character bible confirmation).\n3. **Panel 7 (Civilizations Falling)** -- Complex vertical montage. May need to be generated in bands and composited.\n4. **Panels 2, 3, 9, 10, 11, 13** -- Standard single-scene panels. Generate in batch.\n\n### Sref Strategy\nAll panels should share a single --sref code extracted from the best upscale of the batch 1 frames. Until that code is extracted, all prompts use --sref TBD as placeholder.\n\n### Narration Voice Direction\nThe narrator is not a character. The narrator is the world itself -- ancient, tired, carrying the weight of having watched this cycle happen before. The voice is:\n- Gender-neutral\n- Rhythmically sparse -- short sentences, no exposition\n- Almost reluctant, as if telling this story costs something\n- Never dramatic -- the events are dramatic enough; the voice is quiet\n\n### Animation Priority (Future)\nPanels ranked by animation value:\n1. Panel 6 (Guard'Daemon wave) -- the most cinematic motion in the sequence\n2. Panel 5 (Divine Conflict) -- divine war chaos\n3. Panel 12 (The Crack) -- crack spreading, red light pulsing\n4. /Users/eviemini/.claude/projects/-Users-eviemini-Documents-code-server-manager/7688d2ab-634b-4274-a664-daabda403e5b/subagents/agent-a96ba54f9bb5452bd.jsonl:{"parentUuid":"3d16019a-82c3-47aa-a135-f4560ff90e6e","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"7688d2ab-634b-4274-a664-daabda403e5b","version":"2.1.63","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a96ba54f9bb5452bd","slug":"vectorized-exploring-steele","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019571FvDSb4AFkfMav1MbBP","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Opening Sequence Proposals — Heroes of Mardov\n     2→\n     3→*Status: Three proposals for OQ-003 (\"What is the opening scene/chapter of the comic?\"). Evie decides.*\n     4→*Source: Full campaign read, style guide, VISION.md, DM notes.*\n     5→*Last updated: 2026-03-02, Director Agent*\n     6→\n     7→---\n     8→\n     9→## Context\n    10→\n    11→The opening sequence is the single most important creative decision in the series' launch. It determines:\n    12→\n    13→- **Who the audience meets first** — and therefore who they bond with\n    14→- **What tone is established** — gothic? adventurous? intimate? mythic?\n    15→- **How much worldbuilding comes before story** — the Mardov trap is over-explaining; the risk is under-explaining\n    16→- **Whether a newcomer stays or scrolls** — you have 30 seconds on TikTok, 2 minutes on YouTube\n    17→\n    18→The three proposals below offer different answers to the same question: *What makes someone who has never heard of Mardov want to know more?*\n    19→\n    20→Each proposal includes: the scene, why it works, what it establishes, how it hooks newcomers, and a panel-by-panel visual sketch.\n    21→\n    22→---\n    23→\n    24→## Proposal A: \"The Letter\"\n    25→\n    26→**Open on prophecy. Close on the heroes who must answer it.**\n    27→\n    28→### The Scene\n    29→\n    30→We don't start with the heroes. We start with Ruth — the Silver Chair of the Diviners Covenant — alone in her cottage, writing a letter by candlelight. Her voice narrates:\n    31→\n    32→> *\"The last war is coming and this world is not ready.\"*\n    33→\n    34→The camera pulls out. Through the window, we see a vast landscape — mountains, forests, frozen wastes, golden fields — all under a sky heavy with stars. Each star pulses, faintly, like a heartbeat.\n    35→\n    36→Cut to: the letter, sealed with wax, placed in the hands of a messenger. The messenger rides. We follow the letter across the world of Mardov in a sweeping travelogue — through the landscapes that will define each season. Each one is beautiful. Each one has something wrong. A shadow where there shouldn't be one. A fire that burns the wrong color. Frost in summer.\n    37→\n    38→The letter arrives. A hand opens it. We don't see the face — just the hands, the letter, and the words we've already heard.\n    39→\n    40→Cut to: a tavern. Strangers. The heroes, before they're heroes. They don't know each other yet. The letter has been read by someone who will bring them together. The music shifts from Ruth's solo cello to a full ensemble.\n    41→\n    42→Title card: **HEROES OF MARDOV**\n    43→\n    44→### Why It Works\n    45→\n    46→- Establishes the *cosmological* scope immediately — this isn't a dungeon crawl, it's an epic\n    47→- Ruth's letter is one of the most resonant source documents (RUTHS note.txt) — real DM writing, not adaptation\n    48→- The visual travelogue introduces the world without info-dumping\n    49→- Ends where the audience expects a story to start — strangers meeting — but with the weight of prophecy already in the air\n    50→- The letter becomes a recurring visual motif (letters appear throughout the campaign)\n    51→\n    52→### What It Establishes\n    53→\n    54→| Element | How |\n    55→|---------|-----|\n    56→| Tone | Gothic. Beautiful. Ominous. Warm candlelight against cosmic darkness. |\n    57→| World | Mardov as a living place — diverse landscapes, each distinct, each threatened. |\n    58→| Stakes | \"The last war is coming.\" From the first sentence, the audience knows something is at risk. |\n    59→| The Prophecy Structure | Ruth is a seer. The world has seers. Fate is known but not controllable. |\n    60→| The Heroes | Introduced last, as strangers — the audience meets them at the same time they meet each other. |\n    61→\n    62→### How It Hooks Newcomers\n    63→\n    64→A mysterious letter. A world that looks like a painting but feels like it's dying. Strangers who are about to become important. The audience wants to know: What is the last war? Why isn't the world ready? Who are these people?\n    65→\n    66→### Panel-by-Panel Visual Sketch (12 panels)\n    67→\n    68→| Panel | Visual | Audio/Text | Motion |\n    69→|-------|--------|------------|--------|\n    70→| 1 | Black. Then a single candle flame, center frame. | Silence. Then: the scratch of a quill. | Flame flickers (animated). |\n    71→| 2 | Pull out: Ruth's hands writing by candlelight. Old hands. Ink on paper. A cottage filled with dried herbs, star charts, crystals. | Ruth (V.O.): \"To whoever finds these words—\" | Candle flicker. Quill moving. |\n    72→| 3 | The letter, close-up. Ruth's handwriting fills the panel. Key phrase visible: \"The last war is coming.\" | Ruth (V.O.): \"The last war is coming and this world is not ready.\" | Ink drying on paper (subtle animation). |\n    73→| 4 | Through the cottage window: a landscape stretching to the horizon. Mountains in the distance. Deep indigo sky. Stars that pulse. | Ruth (V.O.): \"Four seals hold the darkness.\" | Stars pulsing (animated). Slow pan across landscape. |\n    74→| 5 | Ruth seals the letter with red wax. The wax imprint is a symbol — perhaps the Diviners Covenant mark. | The sound of hot wax dropping. A press. | Wax pooling and pressing (animated). |\n    75→| 6 | A messenger on horseback, letter in a satchel. They ride out of the cottage at dawn. | Music begins — a single cello. | Horse galloping, cloak billowing. |\n    76→| 7 | Wide shot: the rider crossing a golden field (Harvest landscape). In the distance, something shimmers — heat haze or magic. Behind: a shadow that doesn't belong to anything visible. | Ruth (V.O.): \"Four seals. Four hearts. Four anchors.\" | Grass blowing, shadow moving. |\n    77→| 8 | Wide shot: the rider passing a frozen lake (Winter landscape). The ice reflects a dragon — not present in the sky. | Ruth (V.O.): \"And the gods who hunger behind them.\" | Ice cracking subtly. Breath misting. |\n    78→| 9 | Wide shot: the rider through a dark forest (Forest landscape). The trees seem to lean in. Faces in the bark, half-formed. | Ruth (V.O.): \"I cannot stop what is coming.\" | Tree branches swaying. Shadow deepening. |\n    79→| 10 | Wide shot: the rider approaching a city built against a mountain (Mountain landscape). Forge-light from the mountain's base. | Ruth (V.O.): \"But I can warn those who might.\" | Forge-light flickering in the mountain. |\n    80→| 11 | Close-up: hands opening the letter. We see only hands — not the face. Firelight. A table. Other people nearby, out of focus. | Ruth (V.O.): \"Find them. Before the darkness does.\" | Letter unfolding. Firelight on paper. |\n    81→| 12 | Wide shot: a tavern interior. Multiple figures, faces shadowed. Each one distinct — a dwarf, a figure in robes, a cloaked silhouette, others. They don't know each other yet. Camera slowly zooms to center frame. | Music swells from cello to full ensemble. | Candle flames throughout the tavern. Ambient motion. |\n    82→| Title | **HEROES OF MARDOV** — text emerges from the center of the composition. | Full theme. | Title animation. |\n    83→\n    84→---\n    85→\n    86→## Proposal B: \"The Forge and the Axe\"\n    87→\n    88→**Open on a character. Close on the world that needs her.**\n    89→\n    90→### The Scene\n    91→\n    92→We start in darkness. The sound of a hammer on metal. Rhythmic. Ancient. The beat of a forge.\n    93→\n    94→Light blooms — orange, gold, white-hot. A dwarven forge. Sparks. Sweat. Hands that know iron. This is Daina's world before the campaign. Before the axe was the War Anchor. Before she knew.\n    95→\n    96→The camera follows the creation of an axe — not Daina's axe (or maybe it is — Evie's call). The forging process is shown with reverence: metal heated, hammered, folded, quenched. Each step is beautiful. The forge is not just a workshop; it's a temple.\n    97→\n    98→Daina is young. Focused. Happy. This is her before the weight lands.\n    99→\n   100→The axe is finished. She holds it up. It catches the light. For a single frame — one frame — the light on the blade shows something. A glyph. A pattern. The audience might not catch it the first time. It's the War Anchor sigil.\n   101→\n   102→Cut to: years later. Daina on the road, axe on her back, approaching a tavern where strangers wait. She's older. Different. The happiness from the forge is gone — or buried. But the axe is the same.\n   103→\n   104→She pushes through the door. Inside: the rest of the party. Strangers who will become family.\n   105→\n   106→Title card: **HEROES OF MARDOV**\n   107→\n   108→### Why It Works\n   109→\n   110→- Opens on a single character — the audience bonds with one person before meeting the world\n   111→- The forge sequence is visually stunning (fire, metal, Castlevania aesthetic, the style guide made manifest)\n   112→- Plants the Anchor mystery in the first 60 seconds, invisible to newcomers, devastating on rewatch\n   113→- The time-skip (young Daina → road Daina) immediately tells the audience: this person has been through things\n   114→- Daina is the War Anchor carrier — arguably the campaign's central character for the adaptation\n   115→\n   116→### What It Establishes\n   117→\n   118→| Element | How |\n   119→|---------|-----|\n   120→| Tone | Gothic. Warm but heavy. Beautiful craftsmanship with darkness beneath. |\n   121→| Character | Daina — a grounded, physical, emotionally complex hero. Not a chosen one; a maker. |\n   122→| The Axe | Central object of the series. Introduced as craft, revealed later as cosmological instrument. |\n   123→| Foreshadowing | The glyph flash. One frame. A promise to the audience that the show rewards attention. |\n   124→| The Heroes | Daina arriving at the tavern implies everyone else is already there. The audience enters through her eyes. |\n   125→\n   126→### How It Hooks Newcomers\n   127→\n   128→A craftsperson making something with her hands. A time skip that says \"years passed and things changed.\" A mysterious flash that says \"there's more to this axe.\" A door opening onto strangers. The audience wants to know: What happened between then and now? What is that axe really? Who are these people?\n   129→\n   130→### Panel-by-Panel Visual Sketch (10 panels)\n   131→\n   132→| Panel | Visual | Audio/Text | Motion |\n   133→|-------|--------|------------|--------|\n   134→| 1 | Black. A rhythmic sound. Clang. Clang. Clang. | Only the hammer. No music. | None — still darkness. |\n   135→| 2 | A single point of orange light. Growing. A forge heating up. | The roar of bellows. | Embers rising (animated). |\n   136→| 3 | Wide shot: the forge. A dwarven workshop carved from stone. Tools on walls. An anvil center frame. A young woman working — Daina, maybe 16-18 in dwarven years. | Hammer rhythm becomes musical. A slow, building percussion. | Sparks. Firelight. Sweat. |\n   137→| 4 | Close-up: metal on anvil. A blade taking shape. Sparks flying. | Hammer rhythm continues. | Sparks flying (animated). Vivid. |\n   138→| 5 | Close-up: Daina's face. Concentrated. Happy. Lit from below by forge-light. This is a person doing what they were born to do. | Percussion builds. A melody enters. | Firelight on skin. |\n   139→| 6 | The blade quenched in water. Steam. The metal singing. | A sustained note — the sound of the quench becomes music. | Steam rising (animated). |\n   140→| 7 | Daina holds the finished axe up. It catches the light. Beautiful. Perfect. | Music peaks. A moment of silence. | Light traveling along the blade. |\n   141→| 8 | **The flash.** One panel — maybe even one second of animation — the light on the blade forms a pattern. A glyph. The War Anchor sigil. Gone before you can read it. | A single, dissonant note. Barely audible. | The glyph appears and vanishes. |\n   142→| 9 | Time skip. A road. Grey sky. A figure walking — Daina, years later. Axe on her back. Same axe. Different person. The happiness is gone. The determination remains. | Music is now a solitary violin. Weathered. Road-worn. | Cloak in wind. Boots on dirt. |\n   143→| 10 | A tavern door. Daina's hand pushes it open. Light and warmth spill out. Inside: silhouettes of the party. Faces not yet visible. | The violin opens into the full theme as the door opens. | Door opening, light pouring out. |\n   144→| Title | **HEROES OF MARDOV** | Full theme. | Title animation. |\n   145→\n   146→---\n   147→\n   148→## Proposal C: \"The Four Seals\"\n   149→\n   150→**Open on the myth. Close on the mortals it's about to consume.**\n   151→\n   152→### The Scene\n   153→\n   154→A creation myth, told visually.\n   155→\n   156→We see Mardov as it was — before the campaign, before the current age. The world in its making. Gods shaping continents. Light splitting into four colors. A voice narrates — not Ruth, not any character we'll meet. An older voice. Maybe the voice of the world itself.\n   157→\n   158→Four locations: A mountain forge. A primeval forest. A frozen throne. A harvest field in autumn. Four Zeniths.\n   159→\n   160→Something goes wrong. The gods war. Darkness rises. Guard'Daemon — the cyclic cataclysm — shown not as a monster but as an event. A wave. A silence. Civilizations falling in rapid succession, each one thinking they'd be the ones to survive.\n   161→\n   162→The four seals are placed. Four gods imprisoned. The world rebuilt on top of their prisons. A fragile peace built on four locks.\n   163→\n   164→Centuries pass in a montage. The seals hold. The world forgets why they were made. Cities grow. People live their lives. The danger fades into myth.\n   165→\n   166→Then: a crack. One of the seals. Almost imperceptible. A sound like ice breaking in a still room.\n   167→\n   168→Cut to: a tavern. Unaware strangers. The heroes, before they're heroes. Living their small lives in a world built on four prisons.\n   169→\n   170→Title card: **HEROES OF MARDOV**\n   171→\n   172→### Why It Works\n   173→\n   174→- Establishes the *entire mythology* in 90 seconds — the audience understands the world's foundation\n   175→- The visual creation myth is pure style-guide material (indigo, gold, crimson, grey stone)\n   176→- Guard'Daemon shown as a cycle, not a villain — immediately sets this apart from generic fantasy\n   177→- The \"crack\" at the end is the series' central dramatic question made into a single sound\n   178→- The transition from cosmic myth to small tavern creates the scale contrast that defines the series\n   179→\n   180→### What It Establishes\n   181→\n   182→| Element | How |\n   183→|---------|-----|\n   184→| Tone | Mythic. Ancient. The weight of deep time. Gothic at the grandest scale. |\n   185→| World | Mardov's entire cosmological structure — four Zeniths, four seals, Guard'Daemon. |\n   186→| Stakes | Civilizations have already died to this threat. The current world doesn't know it's next. |\n   187→| The Cycle | Guard'Daemon is not a person. It's a pattern. That's worse. |\n   188→| The Heroes | Introduced as tiny figures in a world that forgot its own danger. Maximum dramatic irony. |\n   189→\n   190→### How It Hooks Newcomers\n   191→\n   192→A creation myth that turns dark. Gods at war. A world rebuilt on four prisons. Centuries of forgetting. Then a crack. The audience wants to know: Which seal cracked? What happens when they break? And who are those people in the tavern who have no idea?\n   193→\n   194→### Panel-by-Panel Visual Sketch (14 panels)\n   195→\n   196→| Panel | Visual | Audio/Text | Motion |\n   197→|-------|--------|------------|--------|\n   198→| 1 | Black. Then: light. A single point of white light in void. | Narrator (V.O.): \"In the beginning, there was only the making.\" | Light expanding (animated). |\n   199→| 2 | The light splits into four colors: gold, green, ice-blue, amber. They spiral outward, forming a world. | Narrator: \"Four forces shaped this world. Four powers held it.\" | Colors spiraling, forming geography. |\n   200→| 3 | Wide shot: Mardov from above. Continents, oceans, mountains. Beautiful. Intact. The four colors visible as faint veins in the landscape. | Music: ethereal, choral, reverent. | Slow rotation. Clouds moving. |\n   201→| 4 | Four locations, rapid sequence: A mountain with fire in its belly. A forest so old the trees have faces. A frozen throne in an empty hall. A harvest field stretching to a golden horizon. | Narrator: \"Four Zeniths. Four hearts. Four seals.\" | Each location has signature atmospheric motion. |\n   202→| 5 | The sky darkens. The colors war — they collide, corrupt, consume. Gods as silhouettes, vast, fighting across the sky. | Narrator: \"And then the gods forgot why they were made.\" | Divine conflict — lightning, fire, shadow. |\n   203→| 6 | Guard'Daemon. Not a creature — a wave of nothing. An absence. Everything it touches ceases. Cities crumble. Light goes out. | Narrator: \"The silence came. As it always had. As it always would.\" | The wave of nothing advancing across the world. |\n   204→| 7 | Rapid montage: civilizations falling. One after another. Different eras, different peoples. All ending the same way. | Narrator: \"This was not the first time.\" | Quick cuts, each showing a different civilization's fall. |\n   205→| 8 | The four seals placed — four gods imprisoned. Each seal a different material: forge-iron, living wood, glacier-ice, autumn stone. | Narrator: \"They sealed the darkness. Not to destroy it — they could not. To delay it.\" | Each seal locking with finality. |\n   206→| 9 | Time-lapse: centuries passing. The seals buried under dirt, cities, forests, snow. The world growing on top of its own prisons. | Narrator: \"The world rebuilt. And forgot.\" | Seasons cycling. Cities growing. Rapid time-lapse. |\n   207→| 10 | A modern-day Mardov. Cities, trade routes, people living lives. No sign of the seals. No memory of the danger. | Music shifts: lighter. Normal. | Market sounds. Life. |\n   208→| 11 | Close-up: one of the seals, deep underground. Still. Silent. Holding. | Silence. | Nothing moves. |\n   209→| 12 | **The crack.** A hairline fracture across the seal's surface. The sound of ice breaking in a quiet room. | A single sharp sound — crack. Then silence. | The crack spreading (animated). |\n   210→| 13 | Pull out: above the seal, on the surface, life continues. No one heard it. No one knows. | A low, barely audible hum — the frequency of something waking up. | Normal surface activity. Oblivious. |\n   211→| 14 | A tavern. Warm light. Strangers at separate tables. They'll meet tonight, for reasons they don't yet understand. None of them heard the crack. | Music: the full theme, beginning quietly, building. | Candle flames. Conversation. Ambient tavern life. |\n   212→| Title | **HEROES OF MARDOV** | Full theme. | Title animation. |\n   213→\n   214→---\n   215→\n   216→## Comparison Matrix\n   217→\n   218→| Criterion | A: \"The Letter\" | B: \"The Forge and the Axe\" | C: \"The Four Seals\" |\n   219→|-----------|-----------------|---------------------------|---------------------|\n   220→| **Opens with** | Prophecy (Ruth) | Character (Daina) | Mythology (the world) |\n   221→| **Tone** | Intimate → epic | Personal → ominous | Mythic → intimate |\n   222→| **Worldbuilding** | Moderate (landscape travelogue) | Low (one forge, one road) | Maximum (full cosmology) |\n   223→| **Character introduction** | All at once (tavern) | One first, then others | All at once (tavern) |\n   224→| **Foreshadowing density** | High (letter content) | Extreme (Anchor glyph) | Moderate (the crack) |\n   225→| **Rewatch value** | Good (letter context deepens) | Excellent (glyph becomes devastating) | Good (knowing which seal cracked) |\n   226→| **TikTok clip potential** | Strong (Ruth's quote is shareable) | Strong (forge sequence is visual) | Moderate (needs context to land) |\n   227→| **Risk** | Could feel like a cold open that doesn't pay off quickly enough | Commits to Daina as \"the\" main character early | Could feel like an info-dump in disguise |\n   228→| **Style guide alignment** | High (candlelight, gothic, atmospheric) | Very high (forge-light, Castlevania, worn texture) | High (mythic scale, indigo/gold palette) |\n   229→| **Production complexity** | Medium (interiors, landscapes, characters) | Low-medium (forge interiors, one character) | High (cosmological visuals, time-lapse, divine conflict) |\n   230→\n   231→---\n   232→\n   233→## Director's Recommendation\n   234→\n   235→**Proposal B (\"The Forge and the Axe\")** is my recommendation for the following reasons:\n   236→\n   237→1. **Character-first storytelling.** VISION.md says \"the pacing and intimacy of a comic.\" Starting with a person making something with her hands is the most intimate possible opening.\n   238→2. **The Anchor glyph.** One frame of foreshadowing that pays off 20+ episodes later. This is the kind of detail that turns viewers into evangelists — \"go back and watch the opening again.\"\n   239→3. **Production feasibility.** A forge interior with one character is the simplest of the three to generate at the quality bar VISION.md demands. It lets us prove the style before attempting cosmic scale.\n   240→4. **Daina as entry point.** Her arc (mortal warrior → carries a god's prison → declares war on the gods) is the most complete character journey in the source material. She's the audience's way in.\n   241→5. **The time-skip.** Showing Daina young/happy and then older/weathered in two panels tells the audience everything about the series' emotional register without a word of exposition.\n   242→\n   243→However: **this is Evie's call (OQ-003).** If the campaign's emotional center is not Daina, or if Evie feels the mythology must come first, Proposal C gives the fullest world introduction. If the prophecy and Ruth's voice are the heart of the story, Proposal A gives the most atmospheric and narratively rich opening.\n   244→\n   245→All three end in the same place: strangers in a tavern, about to become heroes. The question is what the audience knows — and feels — before they get there.\n   246→\n   247→---\n   248→\n   249→## Open Questions for Evie (Related to Opening)\n   250→\n   251→1. Is Daina the right \"first face\" of the series? Or is another PC the emotional center?\n   252→2. Does Ruth appear in person, or only through her letter?\n   253→3. Should the opening sequence be the same every episode (like a TV intro), or unique to Episode 1?\n   254→4. Is there a moment from the actual first session (Chapter 0-1) that MUST be in the opening?\n   255→5. Does the series begin before the campaign (backstories), at the campaign start (Chapter 0), or *in medias res* (a later moment, then flashback)?\n   256→\n   257→---\n   258→\n   259→*Three proposals. One decision. Evie's table, Evie's call.*\n   260→"}]},"uuid":"6f9efc77-9f77-4117-8734-4a0ca1857699","timestamp":"2026-03-04T22:43:34.311Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3d16019a-82c3-47aa-a135-f4560ff90e6e"}

/Users/eviemini/.claude/projects/-Users-eviemini-Documents-code-server-manager/64782e4f-3fce-4874-ab1c-42c5d0a717d9.jsonl:{"parentUuid":"79505723-20bc-4479-9e0a-3578fe2eeb9a","isSidechain":false,"userType":"external","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"64782e4f-3fce-4874-ab1c-42c5d0a717d9","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"main","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01AafyZrhC1vcHDx3YvCW7V8","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# TODO — Story Dreamer v13\n     2→\n     3→## How This App Is Used (READ FIRST)\n     4→\n     5→Story Dreamer is the command center for **Mardov**, an automated art-to-video pipeline. Understanding this pipeline is critical — most items below relate to how data flows through it.\n     6→\n     7→### The Pipeline\n     8→\n     9→```\n    10→Screenplay (100+ scenes)\n    11→    |\n    12→MJ Hopper (daemon) -> generates 4-grid images from prompts\n    13→    |\n    14→Review System -> scores grids 1-5, assigns verdicts (APPROVE/ADJUST/REGEN)\n    15→    |\n    16→Verdict-to-Refine (daemon) -> crops best quadrant from approved grids -> refined/ images\n    17→    |\n    18→Kling Seeder (daemon) -> queues refined images for video generation\n    19→    |\n    20→Kling Hopper (daemon) -> generates 5s ambient animation videos via Playwright\n    21→    |\n    22→Episode Assembler (daemon) -> groups videos by episode, sorts by panel number\n    23→    |\n    24→Canvas Export -> produces canvas-export.json with everything\n    25→    |\n    26→THIS APP -> imports canvas-export.json, displays galleries, plays episodes\n    27→```\n    28→\n    29→### Data Format\n    30→\n    31→The app imports a single JSON file (`canvas-export.json`) containing:\n    32→- `meta` — project title, version, poll URL for live updates\n    33→- `scenes[]` — screenplay scenes with headings, action lines, image prompts\n    34→- `mjGrids[]` — MJ 4-grid images with quadrant selection state\n    35→- `mjQueue[]` / `klingQueue[]` — pipeline status for each generation job\n    36→- `episodes[]` — assembled video episodes with clips sorted by panel number\n    37→- `comicPanels[]` — webcomic panels with dialogue\n    38→- `reviewQueue[]` — items flagged for human review\n    39→\n    40→### Asset Serving\n    41→\n    42→A CORS HTTP server runs at `http://localhost:9099` rooted at the Mardov project directory. All asset URLs in `canvas-export.json` are relative to this root. The canonical export location is `production/canvas-export.json`.\n    43→\n    44→### Iteration Data\n    45→\n    46→Many panels have multiple MJ generations (rerolls, versions). These share a base label with suffixes like `-reroll-1`, `-reroll-2`, `-v2`, `-v3`. The app groups these into collapsed cards and shows all iterations in the fullscreen detail modal with quadrant voting and star ratings.\n    47→\n    48→---\n    49→\n    50→## Protocol\n    51→\n    52→Follow these rules exactly:\n    53→\n    54→1. **One item at a time.** Pick the first unchecked `- [ ]` item. Do not skip ahead.\n    55→2. **Read the file mentioned in the item first.** Every item names a file and often a line number. Open it before changing anything.\n    56→3. **Make the minimal fix described.** Each item has a \"Fix:\" instruction. Do exactly that. No refactoring, no bonus improvements, no renaming, no added comments.\n    57→4. **Do not touch unrelated code.** Don't \"clean up\" other files while you're there.\n    58→5. **Do not rewrite this file.** Do not rename sections, add emojis, change the structure, or mark the whole file as completed. Only change `- [ ]` to `- [x]` for the item you just finished.\n    59→6. **Commit after each item** with a message like: `fix: correct quadrant crop CSS in detail modal`\n    60→7. **Move to the next unchecked item.** Repeat from step 1.\n    61→8. **When all items are done**, go to the \"Suggest Improvements\" section at the bottom and add your own ideas there.\n    62→\n    63→---\n    64→\n    65→## Items\n    66→\n    67→- [x] **Wire PresentationMode into VideoViewer and AnimaticViewer**: `PresentationMode.tsx` was created in v12 but never connected to the app. Fix: In `VideoViewer.tsx`, add a \"Present\" button in the toolbar area that sets a `presenting` state to true. When `presenting` is true, render `<PresentationMode>` wrapping the current video content, passing the video element ref, prev/next/play-pause callbacks, and an `onExit` callback that sets `presenting` back to false. Do the same in `AnimaticViewer.tsx` if it exists. In `Index.tsx`, add a global `F` key listener (only when the Video or Animatic tab is active) that triggers presentation mode. The `F` listener should not fire when an input or textarea is focused. Files: `src/components/VideoViewer.tsx`, `src/pages/Index.tsx`, `src/components/PresentationMode.tsx` (read only — do not modify).\n    68→\n    69→- [x] **Keyboard shortcuts for gallery curation**: The gallery requires mouse clicks for every action — selecting quadrants, approving/rejecting, navigating between grids. Fix: Add keyboard shortcuts that work when the gallery tab is active and no input is focused. Arrow keys (Left/Right) navigate between grid cards, highlighting the current one with a ring. Number keys 1-4 select the corresponding quadrant on the highlighted grid. `A` key approves (sets verdict to APPROVE), `R` key rejects (REGEN). `Space` opens the detail modal for the highlighted grid. `Escape` closes the detail modal. Track the currently highlighted grid index in state. Scroll the highlighted card into view when navigating with arrows. Show a small keyboard shortcut hint overlay (toggleable with `?` key) listing all shortcuts. Files: `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx`, `src/components/gallery/GridCard.tsx` (add highlighted prop styling).\n    70→\n    71→- [x] **Batch approve/reject with undo**: Currently grids can only be curated one at a time. Fix: Add multi-select capability — `Shift+Click` selects a range from the last selected to the clicked grid, `Cmd+Click` (or `Ctrl+Click`) toggles individual selection. Selected grids get a blue ring overlay. When grids are selected, show a floating action bar at the bottom of the gallery with \"Approve All\", \"Reject All\", and \"Clear Selection\" buttons plus a count badge (\"12 selected\"). Actions apply the verdict to all selected grids at once and clear the selection. After a batch action, show an undo toast (bottom-right, auto-dismiss after 8 seconds) that reverts all verdicts to their previous values. Track previous verdicts in a ref so undo doesn't trigger re-renders until clicked. Files: `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx` (selection state, action bar, undo logic), `src/components/gallery/GridCard.tsx` (selected visual state).\n    72→\n    73→- [x] **Grid comparison side-by-side with slider**: When reviewing iterations of the same panel, there's no way to directly compare two grids pixel-by-pixel. Fix: In `GridDetailModal.tsx`, when the current grid has iterations (the iteration sidebar shows multiple versions), add a \"Compare\" button in the modal toolbar. Clicking it enters compare mode: the modal splits into two panes with a draggable vertical divider. The left pane shows the currently selected iteration, the right pane shows the next iteration (or the user can click any iteration thumbnail to assign it to either pane). The divider is a thin vertical line with a drag handle — dragging it resizes the two panes proportionally. Both panes zoom/scroll in sync. Pressing `Escape` or clicking \"Compare\" again exits compare mode. Files: `src/components/gallery/GridDetailModal.tsx` (compare mode state, split pane layout, drag handler).\n    74→\n    75→- [x] **Episode-level cumulative progress bar**: In the Episode/Animatic tab, there's no visual indicator of how complete each episode is across the pipeline (MJ grids done vs pending, Kling videos done vs pending). Fix: Above each episode's filmstrip/clip list, render a thin horizontal progress bar (8px tall, rounded) that shows cumulative pipeline status. The bar is divided into colored segments: green for fully done (grid + video complete), amber for in-progress (grid done, video pending/generating), gray for pending (grid not yet done). Calculate percentages from the episode's panels cross-referenced against `mjQueue` and `klingQueue` status. The bar should be clickable — clicking a segment scrolls to and highlights the first panel in that status category. Show a tooltip on hover with exact counts (\"12 done, 5 in progress, 3 pending\"). Files: `src/components/VideoViewer.tsx` (or `EpisodeViewer.tsx` if it exists), new `src/components/EpisodeProgressBar.tsx`.\n    76→\n    77→---\n    78→\n    79→## Suggest Improvements\n    80→\n    81→When you finish all items above, add your own suggestions here. For each suggestion:\n    82→- Describe the problem or opportunity\n    83→- Name the specific file(s) involved\n    84→- Explain what you'd change and why\n    85→- Estimate complexity (small / medium / large)\n    86→\n    87→Write them as unchecked items in the same format as above. Do NOT implement them — just describe them.\n    88→\n    89→- [ ] **GridDetailModal is 335+ lines — extract sub-components**: The modal has grown with compare mode, quadrant tiles, rating, prompt, refined preview, and keyboard hints all in one file. Fix: Extract `QuadrantTiles`, `StarRating`, `PromptSection`, and `RefinedPreview` into separate files under `src/components/gallery/`. The modal file should be a thin layout shell that composes these. This improves readability and testability. Files: `src/components/gallery/GridDetailModal.tsx` → split into 4-5 files. Complexity: medium.\n    90→\n    91→- [ ] **Verdict badges on grid cards**: After adding the verdict system (`APPROVE`/`REGEN`/`ADJUST`), grid cards don't show their verdict visually. Fix: In `GridCard.tsx`, add a small colored badge (green for APPROVE, red for REGEN, amber for ADJUST) in the bottom-left corner of the card image when `grid.verdict` is set. This gives immediate visual feedback during curation sweeps. Files: `src/components/gallery/GridCard.tsx`. Complexity: small.\n    92→\n    93→- [ ] **Persist batch verdicts to auto-save**: The verdict field is in memory state only. When the page reloads, verdicts are lost. Fix: Include `verdict` in the auto-save serialization in `Index.tsx` and ensure `importGrids` in `useMJGridStore` preserves existing verdicts when merging. Files: `src/pages/Index.tsx`, `src/hooks/useMJGridStore.ts`. Complexity: small.\n    94→\n    95→- [ ] **Gallery keyboard shortcut overlay**: The `?` key opens the global shortcut help but doesn't include gallery-specific shortcuts (1-4 quadrant, A/R verdict, N next unpicked, Shift+Click batch). Fix: Add a gallery-specific section to `KeyboardShortcutHelp.tsx` that appears when the gallery tab is active, or render a separate mini-overlay inside `GalleryViewer.tsx`. Files: `src/components/KeyboardShortcutHelp.tsx` or `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx`. Complexity: small.\n    96→\n    97→- [ ] **EpisodeProgressBar in the episode sidebar**: Currently the progress bar only appears above the main viewer area. Each episode in the sidebar should also show a mini progress bar (3px, inline) below the episode thumbnail so the user can scan completion status at a glance without clicking each one. Files: `src/components/VideoViewer.tsx` (EpisodeSidebar sub-component), `src/components/EpisodeProgressBar.tsx` (add a `mini` variant prop). Complexity: small.\n    98→"}]},"uuid":"7e5a0069-d347-4740-a3c5-faf895aee5b1","timestamp":"2026-03-09T05:02:08.667Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/story-dreamer/TODO.md","content":"# TODO — Story Dreamer v13\n\n## How This App Is Used (READ FIRST)\n\nStory Dreamer is the command center for **Mardov**, an automated art-to-video pipeline. Understanding this pipeline is critical — most items below relate to how data flows through it.\n\n### The Pipeline\n\n```\nScreenplay (100+ scenes)\n    |\nMJ Hopper (daemon) -> generates 4-grid images from prompts\n    |\nReview System -> scores grids 1-5, assigns verdicts (APPROVE/ADJUST/REGEN)\n    |\nVerdict-to-Refine (daemon) -> crops best quadrant from approved grids -> refined/ images\n    |\nKling Seeder (daemon) -> queues refined images for video generation\n    |\nKling Hopper (daemon) -> generates 5s ambient animation videos via Playwright\n    |\nEpisode Assembler (daemon) -> groups videos by episode, sorts by panel number\n    |\nCanvas Export -> produces canvas-export.json with everything\n    |\nTHIS APP -> imports canvas-export.json, displays galleries, plays episodes\n```\n\n### Data Format\n\nThe app imports a single JSON file (`canvas-export.json`) containing:\n- `meta` — project title, version, poll URL for live updates\n- `scenes[]` — screenplay scenes with headings, action lines, image prompts\n- `mjGrids[]` — MJ 4-grid images with quadrant selection state\n- `mjQueue[]` / `klingQueue[]` — pipeline status for each generation job\n- `episodes[]` — assembled video episodes with clips sorted by panel number\n- `comicPanels[]` — webcomic panels with dialogue\n- `reviewQueue[]` — items flagged for human review\n\n### Asset Serving\n\nA CORS HTTP server runs at `http://localhost:9099` rooted at the Mardov project directory. All asset URLs in `canvas-export.json` are relative to this root. The canonical export location is `production/canvas-export.json`.\n\n### Iteration Data\n\nMany panels have multiple MJ generations (rerolls, versions). These share a base label with suffixes like `-reroll-1`, `-reroll-2`, `-v2`, `-v3`. The app groups these into collapsed cards and shows all iterations in the fullscreen detail modal with quadrant voting and star ratings.\n\n---\n\n## Protocol\n\nFollow these rules exactly:\n\n1. **One item at a time.** Pick the first unchecked `- [ ]` item. Do not skip ahead.\n2. **Read the file mentioned in the item first.** Every item names a file and often a line number. Open it before changing anything.\n3. **Make the minimal fix described.** Each item has a \"Fix:\" instruction. Do exactly that. No refactoring, no bonus improvements, no renaming, no added comments.\n4. **Do not touch unrelated code.** Don't \"clean up\" other files while you're there.\n5. **Do not rewrite this file.** Do not rename sections, add emojis, change the structure, or mark the whole file as completed. Only change `- [ ]` to `- [x]` for the item you just finished.\n6. **Commit after each item** with a message like: `fix: correct quadrant crop CSS in detail modal`\n7. **Move to the next unchecked item.** Repeat from step 1.\n8. **When all items are done**, go to the \"Suggest Improvements\" section at the bottom and add your own ideas there.\n\n---\n\n## Items\n\n- [x] **Wire PresentationMode into VideoViewer and AnimaticViewer**: `PresentationMode.tsx` was created in v12 but never connected to the app. Fix: In `VideoViewer.tsx`, add a \"Present\" button in the toolbar area that sets a `presenting` state to true. When `presenting` is true, render `<PresentationMode>` wrapping the current video content, passing the video element ref, prev/next/play-pause callbacks, and an `onExit` callback that sets `presenting` back to false. Do the same in `AnimaticViewer.tsx` if it exists. In `Index.tsx`, add a global `F` key listener (only when the Video or Animatic tab is active) that triggers presentation mode. The `F` listener should not fire when an input or textarea is focused. Files: `src/components/VideoViewer.tsx`, `src/pages/Index.tsx`, `src/components/PresentationMode.tsx` (read only — do not modify).\n\n- [x] **Keyboard shortcuts for gallery curation**: The gallery requires mouse clicks for every action — selecting quadrants, approving/rejecting, navigating between grids. Fix: Add keyboard shortcuts that work when the gallery tab is active and no input is focused. Arrow keys (Left/Right) navigate between grid cards, highlighting the current one with a ring. Number keys 1-4 select the corresponding quadrant on the highlighted grid. `A` key approves (sets verdict to APPROVE), `R` key rejects (REGEN). `Space` opens the detail modal for the highlighted grid. `Escape` closes the detail modal. Track the currently highlighted grid index in state. Scroll the highlighted card into view when navigating with arrows. Show a small keyboard shortcut hint overlay (toggleable with `?` key) listing all shortcuts. Files: `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx`, `src/components/gallery/GridCard.tsx` (add highlighted prop styling).\n\n- [x] **Batch approve/reject with undo**: Currently grids can only be curated one at a time. Fix: Add multi-select capability — `Shift+Click` selects a range from the last selected to the clicked grid, `Cmd+Click` (or `Ctrl+Click`) toggles individual selection. Selected grids get a blue ring overlay. When grids are selected, show a floating action bar at the bottom of the gallery with \"Approve All\", \"Reject All\", and \"Clear Selection\" buttons plus a count badge (\"12 selected\"). Actions apply the verdict to all selected grids at once and clear the selection. After a batch action, show an undo toast (bottom-right, auto-dismiss after 8 seconds) that reverts all verdicts to their previous values. Track previous verdicts in a ref so undo doesn't trigger re-renders until clicked. Files: `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx` (selection state, action bar, undo logic), `src/components/gallery/GridCard.tsx` (selected visual state).\n\n- [x] **Grid comparison side-by-side with slider**: When reviewing iterations of the same panel, there's no way to directly compare two grids pixel-by-pixel. Fix: In `GridDetailModal.tsx`, when the current grid has iterations (the iteration sidebar shows multiple versions), add a \"Compare\" button in the modal toolbar. Clicking it enters compare mode: the modal splits into two panes with a draggable vertical divider. The left pane shows the currently selected iteration, the right pane shows the next iteration (or the user can click any iteration thumbnail to assign it to either pane). The divider is a thin vertical line with a drag handle — dragging it resizes the two panes proportionally. Both panes zoom/scroll in sync. Pressing `Escape` or clicking \"Compare\" again exits compare mode. Files: `src/components/gallery/GridDetailModal.tsx` (compare mode state, split pane layout, drag handler).\n\n- [x] **Episode-level cumulative progress bar**: In the Episode/Animatic tab, there's no visual indicator of how complete each episode is across the pipeline (MJ grids done vs pending, Kling videos done vs pending). Fix: Above each episode's filmstrip/clip list, render a thin horizontal progress bar (8px tall, rounded) that shows cumulative pipeline status. The bar is divided into colored segments: green for fully done (grid + video complete), amber for in-progress (grid done, video pending/generating), gray for pending (grid not yet done). Calculate percentages from the episode's panels cross-referenced against `mjQueue` and `klingQueue` status. The bar should be clickable — clicking a segment scrolls to and highlights the first panel in that status category. Show a tooltip on hover with exact counts (\"12 done, 5 in progress, 3 pending\"). Files: `src/components/VideoViewer.tsx` (or `EpisodeViewer.tsx` if it exists), new `src/components/EpisodeProgressBar.tsx`.\n\n---\n\n## Suggest Improvements\n\nWhen you finish all items above, add your own suggestions here. For each suggestion:\n- Describe the problem or opportunity\n- Name the specific file(s) involved\n- Explain what you'd change and why\n- Estimate complexity (small / medium / large)\n\nWrite them as unchecked items in the same format as above. Do NOT implement them — just describe them.\n\n- [ ] **GridDetailModal is 335+ lines — extract sub-components**: The modal has grown with compare mode, quadrant tiles, rating, prompt, refined preview, and keyboard hints all in one file. Fix: Extract `QuadrantTiles`, `StarRating`, `PromptSection`, and `RefinedPreview` into separate files under `src/components/gallery/`. The modal file should be a thin layout shell that composes these. This improves readability and testability. Files: `src/components/gallery/GridDetailModal.tsx` → split into 4-5 files. Complexity: medium.\n\n- [ ] **Verdict badges on grid cards**: After adding the verdict system (`APPROVE`/`REGEN`/`ADJUST`), grid cards don't show their verdict visually. Fix: In `GridCard.tsx`, add a small colored badge (green for APPROVE, red for REGEN, amber for ADJUST) in the bottom-left corner of the card image when `grid.verdict` is set. This gives immediate visual feedback during curation sweeps. Files: `src/components/gallery/GridCard.tsx`. Complexity: small.\n\n- [ ] **Persist batch verdicts to auto-save**: The verdict field is in memory state only. When the page reloads, verdicts are lost. Fix: Include `verdict` in the auto-save serialization in `Index.tsx` and ensure `importGrids` in `useMJGridStore` preserves existing verdicts when merging. Files: `src/pages/Index.tsx`, `src/hooks/useMJGridStore.ts`. Complexity: small.\n\n- [ ] **Gallery keyboard shortcut overlay**: The `?` key opens the global shortcut help but doesn't include gallery-specific shortcuts (1-4 quadrant, A/R verdict, N next unpicked, Shift+Click batch). Fix: Add a gallery-specific section to `KeyboardShortcutHelp.tsx` that appears when the gallery tab is active, or render a separate mini-overlay inside `GalleryViewer.tsx`. Files: `src/components/KeyboardShortcutHelp.tsx` or `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx`. Complexity: small.\n\n- [ ] **EpisodeProgressBar in the episode sidebar**: Currently the progress bar only appears above the main viewer area. Each episode in the sidebar should also show a mini progress bar (3px, inline) below the episode thumbnail so the user can scan completion status at a glance without clicking each one. Files: `src/components/VideoViewer.tsx` (EpisodeSidebar sub-component), `src/components/EpisodeProgressBar.tsx` (add a `mini` variant prop). Complexity: small.\n","numLines":98,"startLine":1,"totalLines":98}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"79505723-20bc-4479-9e0a-3578fe2eeb9a"}

Understanding this pipeline is critical — most items below relate to how data flows through it.\n\n### The Pipeline\n\n```\nScreenplay (100+ scenes)\n    |\nMJ Hopper (daemon) -> generates 4-grid images from prompts\n    |\nReview System -> scores grids 1-5, assigns verdicts (APPROVE/ADJUST/REGEN)\n    |\nVerdict-to-Refine (daemon) -> crops best quadrant from approved grids -> refined/ images\n    |\nKling Seeder (daemon) -> queues refined images for video generation\n    |\nKling Hopper (daemon) -> generates 5s ambient animation videos via Playwright\n    |\nEpisode Assembler (daemon) -> groups videos by episode, sorts by panel number\n    |\nCanvas Export -> produces canvas-export.json with everything\n    |\nTHIS APP -> imports canvas-export.json, displays galleries, plays episodes\n```\n\n### Data Format\n\nThe app imports a single JSON file (`canvas-export.json`) containing:\n- `meta` — project title, version, poll URL for live updates\n- `scenes[]` — screenplay scenes with headings, action lines, image prompts\n- `mjGrids[]` — MJ 4-grid images with quadrant selection state\n- `mjQueue[]` / `klingQueue[]` — pipeline status for each generation job\n- `episodes[]` — assembled video episodes with clips sorted by panel number\n- `comicPanels[]` — webcomic panels with dialogue\n- `reviewQueue[]` — items flagged for human review\n\n### Asset Serving\n\nA CORS HTTP server runs at `http://localhost:9099` rooted at the Mardov project directory. All asset URLs in `canvas-export.json` are relative to this root. The canonical export location is `production/canvas-export.json`.\n\n### Iteration Data\n\nMany panels have multiple MJ generations (rerolls, versions). These share a base label with suffixes like `-reroll-1`, `-reroll-2`, `-v2`, `-v3`. The app groups these into collapsed cards and shows all iterations in the fullscreen detail modal with quadrant voting and star ratings.\n\n---\n\n## Protocol\n\nFollow these rules exactly:\n\n1. **One item at a time.** Pick the first unchecked `- [ ]` item. Do not skip ahead.\n2. **Read the file mentioned in the item first.** Every item names a file and often a line number. Open it before changing anything.\n3. **Make the minimal fix described.** Each item has a \"Fix:\" instruction. Do exactly that. No refactoring, no bonus improvements, no renaming, no added comments.\n4. **Do not touch unrelated code.** Don't \"clean up\" other files while you're there.\n5. **Do not rewrite this file.** Do not rename sections, add emojis, change the structure, or mark the whole file as completed. Only change `- [ ]` to `- [x]` for the item you just finished.\n6. **Commit after each item** with a message like: `fix: correct quadrant crop CSS in detail modal`\n7. **Move to the next unchecked item.** Repeat from step 1.\n8. **When all items are done**, go to the \"Suggest Improvements\" section at the bottom and add your own ideas there.\n\n---\n\n## Items\n\n- [ ] **Bulk re-export trigger**: The canvas-export pipeline must be triggered manually from the command line. Fix: Add a \"Re-export\" button in SettingsDrawer (or the header area near the import button). When clicked, POST to a configurable webhook URL stored in settings (`exportWebhookUrl`). Add the `exportWebhookUrl` field to the settings store. On success (2xx response), show a success toast and auto-reimport the fresh `canvas-export.json` from the configured asset base URL after a 3-second delay. On failure, show an error toast with the status code. The button should show a spinner while the request is in flight. Files: `src/components/SettingsDrawer.tsx` (add webhook URL field + button), `src/hooks/useSettingsStore.ts` (add `exportWebhookUrl` to settings type and defaults).\n\n- [ ] **Persist gallery filters in URL params**: Changing status/episode filters in the gallery resets on page refresh. Fix: In `GalleryViewer.tsx`, sync `statusFilter`, `episodeFilter`, and `groupIterations` to URL search params using `useSearchParams` from `react-router-dom` (or `URLSearchParams` + `window.history.replaceState` if no router is present). On mount, read initial values from URL params. On change, update URL params without navigation. Param names: `status`, `episode`, `group` (boolean \"1\"/\"0\"). This enables deep links to filtered views and browser back/forward to navigate filter history. Files: `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx`.\n\n- [ ] **Export gallery verdicts as pipeline-compatible JSON**: After curating grids, there's no way to export the verdicts back to the pipeline. Fix: Add an \"Export Verdicts\" button in the gallery toolbar (next to the size toggle). When clicked, generate a JSON file containing an array of objects, one per grid that has a verdict or selected quadrant. Each object: `{ label: string, selectedQuadrant: number | null, rating: number | null, verdict: \"APPROVE\" | \"ADJUST\" | \"REGEN\" | null, timestamp: string }`. The `label` field is `grid.id` with the `mj-` prefix stripped. `selectedQuadrant` is the 1-indexed quadrant number (or null if none selected). Download the file as `verdicts-{ISO-timestamp}.json`. Files: `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx` (button + export logic).\n\n- [ ] **Scene-to-asset traceability view**: In the Screenplay tab, there's no way to see which MJ grids and Kling videos are associated with a scene. Fix: Add a collapsible \"Assets\" panel below each scene card in `ScreenplayViewer.tsx`. The panel shows: (1) thumbnails of all MJ grids whose `sceneId` matches the scene's heading/label, with quadrant selection checkmarks and verdict badges; (2) any Kling video thumbnails linked to that scene. Show a mini progress indicator: \"2 grids, 1 video\" or \"0 assets\". The panel is collapsed by default and toggles with a click on an \"Assets (N)\" label. Pass `mjGrids` and `klingQueue` props from `Index.tsx` if not already passed. Files: `src/components/ScreenplayViewer.tsx`, `src/pages/Index.tsx` (ensure props are passed).\n\n- [ ] **Lazy-load GridDetailModal and code-split heavy components**: `GridDetailModal`, `GridComparePane`, and `PresentationMode` are imported eagerly even though they're only shown on user interaction. Fix: In `GalleryViewer.tsx`, replace the static import of `GridDetailModal` with `React.lazy(() => import(...))` and wrap the rendered modal in `<Suspense fallback={<ModalSkeleton />}>`. Create a simple `ModalSkeleton` component inline (a centered spinner or pulsing rectangle). Do the same for `PresentationMode` in `VideoViewer.tsx` and `AnimaticViewer.tsx`. In `GridDetailModal.tsx`, replace the static import of `GridComparePane` with a lazy import. Files: `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx`, `src/components/VideoViewer.tsx`, `src/components/AnimaticViewer.tsx`, `src/components/gallery/GridDetailModal.tsx`.\n\n---\n\n## Suggest Improvements\n\nWhen you finish all items above, add your own suggestions here. For each suggestion:\n- Describe the problem or opportunity\n- Name the specific file(s) involved\n- Explain what you'd change and why\n- Estimate complexity (small / medium / large)\n\nWrite them as unchecked items in the same format as above. Do NOT implement them — just describe them.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":1,"oldLines":4,"newStart":1,"newLines":4,"lines":["-# TODO — Story Dreamer v13","+# TODO — Story Dreamer v14"," "," ## How This App Is Used (READ FIRST)"," "]},{"oldStart":64,"oldLines":15,"newStart":64,"newLines":15,"lines":[" "," ## Items"," ","-- [x] **Wire PresentationMode into VideoViewer and AnimaticViewer**: `PresentationMode.tsx` was created in v12 but never connected to the app. Fix: In `VideoViewer.tsx`, add a \"Present\" button in the toolbar area that sets a `presenting` state to true. When `presenting` is true, render `<PresentationMode>` wrapping the current video content, passing the video element ref, prev/next/play-pause callbacks, and an `onExit` callback that sets `presenting` back to false. The `F` listener should not fire when an input or textarea is focused. Number keys 1-4 select the corresponding quadrant on the highlighted grid. `A` key approves (sets verdict to APPROVE), `R` key rejects (REGEN). `Space` opens the detail modal for the highlighted grid. `Escape` closes the detail modal. Track the currently highlighted grid index in state. Scroll the highlighted card into view when navigating with arrows. Show a small keyboard shortcut hint overlay (toggleable with `?` key) listing all shortcuts. On mount, read initial values from URL params. On change, update URL params without navigation. Param names: `status`, `episode`, `group` (boolean \"1\"/\"0\"). This enables deep links to filtered views and browser back/forward to navigate filter history. Actions apply the verdict to all selected grids at once and clear the selection. After a batch action, show an undo toast (bottom-right, auto-dismiss after 8 seconds) that reverts all verdicts to their previous values. Track previous verdicts in a ref so undo doesn't trigger re-renders until clicked. Files: `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx` (selection state, action bar, undo logic), `src/components/gallery/GridCard.tsx` (selected visual state).","+- [ ] **Export gallery verdicts as pipeline-compatible JSON**: After curating grids, there's no way to export the verdicts back to the pipeline. The left pane shows the currently selected iteration, the right pane shows the next iteration (or the user can click any iteration thumbnail to assign it to either pane). The divider is a thin vertical line with a drag handle — dragging it resizes the two panes proportionally. Both panes zoom/scroll in sync. Pressing `Escape` or clicking \"Compare\" again exits compare mode. Files: `src/components/VideoViewer.tsx` (EpisodeSidebar sub-component), `src/components/EpisodeProgressBar.tsx` (add a `mini` variant prop). Complexity: small."]}],"originalFile":"# TODO — Story Dreamer v13\n\n## How This App Is Used (READ FIRST)\n\nStory Dreamer is the command center for **Mardov**, an automated art-to-video pipeline. Understanding this pipeline is critical — most items below relate to how data flows through it.\n\n### The Pipeline\n\n```\nScreenplay (100+ scenes)\n    |\nMJ Hopper (daemon) -> generates 4-grid images from prompts\n    |\nReview System -> scores grids 1-5, assigns verdicts (APPROVE/ADJUST/REGEN)\n    |\nVerdict-to-Refine (daemon) -> crops best quadrant from approved grids -> refined/ images\n    |\nKling Seeder (daemon) -> queues refined images for video generation\n    |\nKling Hopper (daemon) -> generates 5s ambient animation videos via Playwright\n    |\nEpisode Assembler (daemon) -> groups videos by episode, sorts by panel number\n    |\nCanvas Export -> produces canvas-export.json with everything\n    |\nTHIS APP -> imports canvas-export.json, displays galleries, plays episodes\n```\n\n### Data Format\n\nThe app imports a single JSON file (`canvas-export.json`) containing:\n- `meta` — project title, version, poll URL for live updates\n- `scenes[]` — screenplay scenes with headings, action lines, image prompts\n- `mjGrids[]` — MJ 4-grid images with quadrant selection state\n- `mjQueue[]` / `klingQueue[]` — pipeline status for each generation job\n- `episodes[]` — assembled video episodes with clips sorted by panel number\n- `comicPanels[]` — webcomic panels with dialogue\n- `reviewQueue[]` — items flagged for human review\n\n### Asset Serving\n\nA CORS HTTP server runs at `http://localhost:9099` rooted at the Mardov project directory. All asset URLs in `canvas-export.json` are relative to this root. The canonical export location is `production/canvas-export.json`.\n\n### Iteration Data\n\nMany panels have multiple MJ generations (rerolls, versions). These share a base label with suffixes like `-reroll-1`, `-reroll-2`, `-v2`, `-v3`. The app groups these into collapsed cards and shows all iterations in the fullscreen detail modal with quadrant voting and star ratings.\n\n---\n\n## Protocol\n\nFollow these rules exactly:\n\n1. **One item at a time.** Pick the first unchecked `- [ ]` item. Do not skip ahead.\n2. **Read the file mentioned in the item first.** Every item names a file and often a line number. Open it before changing anything.\n3. **Make the minimal fix described.** Each item has a \"Fix:\" instruction. Do exactly that. No refactoring, no bonus improvements, no renaming, no added comments.\n4. **Do not touch unrelated code.** Don't \"clean up\" other files while you're there.\n5. **Do not rewrite this file.** Do not rename sections, add emojis, change the structure, or mark the whole file as completed. Only change `- [ ]` to `- [x]` for the item you just finished.\n6. **Commit after each item** with a message like: `fix: correct quadrant crop CSS in detail modal`\n7. **Move to the next unchecked item.** Repeat from step 1.\n8. **When all items are done**, go to the \"Suggest Improvements\" section at the bottom and add your own ideas there.\n\n---\n\n## Items\n\n- [x] **Wire PresentationMode into VideoViewer and AnimaticViewer**: `PresentationMode.tsx` was created in v12 but never connected to the app. Fix: In `VideoViewer.tsx`, add a \"Present\" button in the toolbar area that sets a `presenting` state to true. When `presenting` is true, render `<PresentationMode>` wrapping the current video content, passing the video element ref, prev/next/play-pause callbacks, and an `onExit` callback that sets `presenting` back to false. Do the same in `AnimaticViewer.tsx` if it exists. In `Index.tsx`, add a global `F` key listener (only when the Video or Animatic tab is active) that triggers presentation mode. The `F` listener should not fire when an input or textarea is focused. Files: `src/components/VideoViewer.tsx`, `src/pages/Index.tsx`, `src/components/PresentationMode.tsx` (read only — do not modify).\n\n- [x] **Keyboard shortcuts for gallery curation**: The gallery requires mouse clicks for every action — selecting quadrants, approving/rejecting, navigating between grids. Fix: Add keyboard shortcuts that work when the gallery tab is active and no input is focused. Arrow keys (Left/Right) navigate between grid cards, highlighting the current one with a ring. Number keys 1-4 select the corresponding quadrant on the highlighted grid. `A` key approves (sets verdict to APPROVE), `R` key rejects (REGEN). `Space` opens the detail modal for the highlighted grid. `Escape` closes the detail modal. Track the currently highlighted grid index in state. Scroll the highlighted card into view when navigating with arrows. Show a small keyboard shortcut hint overlay (toggleable with `?` key) listing all shortcuts. Files: `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx`, `src/components/gallery/GridCard.tsx` (add highlighted prop styling).\n\n- [x] **Batch approve/reject with undo**: Currently grids can only be curated one at a time. Fix: Add multi-select capability — `Shift+Click` selects a range from the last selected to the clicked grid, `Cmd+Click` (or `Ctrl+Click`) toggles individual selection. Selected grids get a blue ring overlay. When grids are selected, show a floating action bar at the bottom of the gallery with \"Approve All\", \"Reject All\", and \"Clear Selection\" buttons plus a count badge (\"12 selected\"). Actions apply the verdict to all selected grids at once and clear the selection. After a batch action, show an undo toast (bottom-right, auto-dismiss after 8 seconds) that reverts all verdicts to their previous values. Track previous verdicts in a ref so undo doesn't trigger re-renders until clicked. Files: `src/components/GalleryViewer.tsx` (selection state, action bar, undo logic), `src/components/gallery/GridCard.tsx` (selected visual state).\n\n- [x] **Grid comparison side-by-side with slider**: When reviewing iterations of the same panel, there's no way to directly compare two grids pixel-by-pixel. Fix: In `GridDetailModal.tsx`, when the current grid has iterations (the iteration sidebar shows multiple versions), add a \"Compare\" button in the modal toolbar. Clicking it enters compare mode: the modal splits into two panes with a draggable vertical divider. The left pane shows the currently selected iteration, the right pane shows the next iteration (or the user can click any iteration thumbnail to assign it to either pane). The divider is a thin vertical line with a drag handle — dragging it resizes the two panes proportionally. Both panes zoom/scroll in sync. Pressing `Escape` or clicking \"Compare\" again exits compare mode. Files: `src/components/gallery/GridDetailModal.tsx` (compare mode state, split pane layout, drag handler).\n\n- [x] **Episode-level cumulative progress bar**: In the Episode/Animatic tab, there's no visual indicator of how complete each episode is across the pipeline (MJ grids done vs pending, Kling videos done vs pending). Fix: Above each episode's filmstrip/clip list, render a thin horizontal progress bar (8px tall, rounded) that shows cumulative pipeline status. The bar is divided into colored segments: green for fully done (grid + video complete), amber for in-progress (grid done, video pending/generating), gray for pending (grid not yet done). Calculate percentages from the episode's panels cross-referenced against `mjQueue` and `klingQueue` status. The bar should be clickable — clicking a segment scrolls to and highlights the first panel in that status category. Show a tooltip on hover with exact counts (\"12 done, 5 in progress, 3 pending\"). Files: `src/components/VideoViewer.tsx` (or `EpisodeViewer.tsx` if it exists), new `src/components/EpisodeProgressBar.tsx`.\n\n---\n\n## Suggest Improvements\n\nWhen you finish all items above, add your own suggestions here. For each suggestion:\n- Describe the problem or opportunity\n- Name the specific file(s) involved\n- Explain what you'd change and why\n- Estimate complexity (small / medium / large)\n\nWrite them as unchecked items in the same format as above. Do NOT implement them — just describe them.\n\n- [ ] **GridDetailModal is 335+ lines — extract sub-components**: The modal has grown with compare mode, quadrant tiles, rating, prompt, refined preview, and keyboard hints all in one file. Fix: Extract `QuadrantTiles`, `StarRating`, `PromptSection`, and `RefinedPreview` into separate files under `src/components/gallery/`. The modal file should be a thin layout shell that composes these. This improves readability and testability. Files: `src/components/gallery/GridDetailModal.tsx` → split into 4-5 files. Complexity: medium.\n\n- [ ] **Verdict badges on grid cards**: After adding the verdict system (`APPROVE`/`REGEN`/`ADJUST`), grid cards don't show their verdict visually. Fix: In `GridCard.tsx`, add a small colored badge (green for APPROVE, red for REGEN, amber for ADJUST) in the bottom-left corner of the card image when `grid.verdict` is set. This gives immediate visual feedback during curation sweeps. Files: `src/components/gallery/GridCard.tsx`. Complexity: small.\n\n- [ ] **Persist batch verdicts to auto-save**: The verdict field is in memory state only. When the page reloads, verdicts are lost. Fix: Include `verdict` in the auto-save serialization in `Index.tsx` and ensure `importGrids` in `useMJGridStore` preserves existing verdicts when merging. Let me give the user a clear report.\n\n---\n\nHere is the full report of what was done and what was found.\n\n## What happened\n\n**Goal**: Test `VAEDecodeTiled` (tile_size 512) at 832x1216 on Tiny's RTX 3060 Ti (8GB VRAM, 16GB RAM, WSL2).\n\n**Setup work performed**:\n1. Fixed stale WSL2 portproxy rules (WSL2 IP changes on every restart)\n2. Restarted Docker (systemd broken on inbox WSL -- used `service docker start` or manual dockerd)\n3. Created test workflow JSON using `VAEDecodeTiled` at 832x1216 with tile_size=512\n5. Tested multiple container configurations:\n   - `--lowvram --force-fp16` (original)\n   - `--lowvram --force-fp16 --cpu-vae`\n   - `--novram --force-fp16`\n   - `--novram --force-fp16 --cpu-vae`\n\n**Results**:\n- **Sampling WORKS**: 20 steps at 832x1216 completed in ~10s at ~2 it/s. The UNet loads fine with `--lowvram` (4897MB in fp16).\n- **VAE decode CRASHES**: The AutoencoderKL load/decode phase crashes the container every time. The crash happens silently (SIGKILL from OOM) with no Python traceback.\n- **One run succeeded**: `Prompt executed in 43.72 seconds` appeared in the logs once, but the output image was not saved to the bind-mounted output directory (crash happened immediately after).\n- **Pre-existing success**: A file `test_vaetiled_832x1216_00001_.png` (832x1216, 1.3MB) already existed in `/opt/comfyui/output/`, proving the workflow CAN succeed on this hardware (likely from a previous session).\n\n**Root cause**: The WSL2 environment is fundamentally unstable:\n- Systemd is broken (inbox WSL on Windows 10 22H2 -- zombie processes)\n- SSH daemon needs manual restart after every crash\n- Portproxy rules need rebuilding after every WSL restart\n- 12GB usable RAM in WSL2 (from 16GB total) is borderline for SDXL with --lowvram\n- GPU OOM kills cascade: crash the container, crash Docker, sometimes crash WSL2 itself\n\n## Container configuration left in place\n\nThe ComfyUI container is currently running with:\n- `--restart unless-stopped`\n- `--lowvram --force-fp16` (set via pre-start.sh override)\n- Bind mounts to `/opt/comfyui/models`, `/output`, `/input`, `/custom_nodes`\n- Pre-start script at `/opt/comfyui/pre-start-custom.sh` that reads CLI args from `/opt/comfyui/cli-args.txt`\n\n## Files on Tiny\n\n| Path | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `/opt/comfyui/cli-args.txt` | CLI args for ComfyUI (currently: `--lowvram --force-fp16`) |\n| `/opt/comfyui/pre-start-custom.sh` | Pre-start script that exports CLI_ARGS |\n| `/mnt/c/Users/evie/setup-final.sh` | Container creation script (can re-run to recreate) |\n| `/opt/comfyui/output/test_vaetiled_832x1216_00001_.png` | Pre-existing successful 832x1216 generation |\n\n## How to test manually\n\nFrom the ComfyUI web UI at `http://192.168.0.121:8188`:\n1. Load a workflow\n2. Replace `VAEDecode` node with `VAEDecodeTiled`\n3. Set `tile_size` to 512 (or 256 for even lower VRAM)\n4. Set resolution to 832x1216\n5. Queue the prompt\n\nOr via API:\n```bash\ncurl -X POST http://192.168.0.121:8188/prompt \\\n  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \\\n  -d @/tmp/comfyui_test_payload.json\n```\n\nThe test payload is at `/tmp/comfyui_test_payload.json` on this Mac.\n\n## Remaining TODOs\n\n1. **Fix WSL2 systemd** -- Install Store WSL (`wsl --install --no-distribution` from admin PowerShell) and reboot. This would fix the zombie processes, make `service docker start` reliable, and prevent most of the instability.\n2. **Increase WSL2 memory** -- Add a `.wslconfig` file in `C:\\Users\\evie\\` with `[wsl2]` section setting `memory=14GB` to give WSL more of the 16GB.\n3. **Test via the GUI** -- The web UI may handle the workflow more reliably than API queuing (it retries on transient failures).\n4. **Consider a smaller SDXL model** -- An SDXL checkpoint with fewer parameters or pruned to fp16 would reduce VRAM pressure.\n\nSources:\n- [ComfyUI OOM Fix Guide](https://diffusedalice.com/articles/comfyui-oom-fix)\n- [ComfyUI Server Config](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/settings/server-config)\n- [Run ComfyUI on 4GB VRAM](https://apatero.com/blog/low-vram-no-problem-running-comfyui-on-budget-hardware)\n- [ComfyUI --cpu-vae Feature Request](https://github.com/comfyanonymous/ComfyUI/issues/182)</result>\n<usage><total_tokens>153527</total_tokens><tool_uses>308</tool_uses><duration_ms>10073287</duration_ms></usage>\n</task-notification>\nFull transcript available at: /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-eviemini-Documents-code-server-manager/5ddb50e5-5309-4daf-bd14-028164075dd4/tasks/ac9aac88dcb6d6d70.output"}
